- safety flags: eye_pressure, transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: The bindu is the finest expression of the Supreme; light belongs to the subtle realm and is a means of one-pointedness, with sound being the essence.
- practice: Sit with head, neck and trunk straight and steady, alert and not drowsy. Shut the eyes comfortably, without turning or pressing the eyeballs. First concentrate the mind by silent repetition of a holy name and by visualising a holy form. Then practise drishti yoga: unite the rays of both eyes on one tiny point in the inner darkness and gaze on it with a mind so intent that all other activity stops. When the mind wanders, bring it back to the point. Do not strain the pupils. Practise daily at fixed hours, ideally very early morning, and keep conduct pure. The divine sight is said to open; once one-pointedness is steady, move to inner sound.
  - passage (method; Aphorism 59, source lines 1112-1195): One-pointedness comes by uniting the rays of both eyes on the focus and gazing with a steady mind; this is drishti yoga.
  - passage (caution; Aphorism 59, source lines 1112-1195): Straining the eyes is unnecessary and harmful.
  - passage (method; Aphorism 91, source lines 1786-1940): Alert, eyes shut comfortably, no turning or pressing of the eyeballs.
  - passage (stages; Translators' appendix, source lines 2255-2369): The appendix says the point eventually blossoms into inner light or visions of light, and one gazes into the middle of the darkness or light seen.

## [snt-sawan-singh-spiritual-gems] Spiritual Gems (letters to American and European disciples)
Sawan Singh (Huzur Maharaj Baba Sawan Singh, 1858-1948); letters 1919-1948; first RSSB book edition c. 1958; later reprints; teacher_manual; rights in_copyright; access partial_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 5, confidence high (Digest only (excerpts); letters are partial.)
- mode: perceptual. He contrasts light seen with the Master's form contemplated from a photograph, which he calls a reflection of one's own mind like a bulb seen after closing the eyes; real light and form appear inside after the attention is withdrawn. He says no attempt should be made to look for light.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: For someone who sees nothing: fix the attention on the darkness in the eye focus and keep peeping into it; the darkness will change into light. Repeat the five names mentally with the attention at the eye centre, unconscious of the body; light is not to be looked for, because the faculty that sees is still outside. Numbness and pain of the limbs are signs of withdrawal. Light is already inside; as concentration nears completion, sparks and fleeting flashes replace darkness. Time scale: lengthen repetition to one and a half hours; some did it by Simran alone. Without having seen the Master, concentrate on candlelight or darkness at the eye focus. Not forced: no pressure on eyes, do not take the soul up by force.
- techniques: sound_or_mantra, concentration_nonvisual, gazing_fixed_point, eye_position, devotion_or_transmission, posture_or_body_lock, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena; phenomena: sparks_or_points, lightning_flashes, stars, sun_disc, moon_disc, white, blue, figures_or_forms, all_pervading_light
- conditions: posture: Any posture in which one can remain long without frequent changes.; gaze_or_eye_position: Attention at the eye focus (Tisra Til); no strain or pressure on the eyes.; setting: Solitary room if possible; can meditate lying down when ill.; duration_and_schedule: One and a half hours repetition and half an hour sound daily; add an hour to the existing hour; three-fourths repetition, one-fourth sound.; prerequisites: Initiation, five holy names, pure conduct.; other: Breathing normal and unforced; ignore pain in heart or throat and numbness.
- stages: darkness, sparks, stars, sun, moon, radiant form: Darkness in the eye centre gives way to sparks and flashes, then steady light, a starry sky, a large star that bursts, the sun and moon in turn, then the Master's radiant form.
- with the light: Fix the attention on the big star until it bursts and pass through it; likewise the sun and moon; look at the light while continuing repetition; do not stop at the stars; absorb in the Master's form.
- obstacles/fixes: Seeing nothing: peep into the darkness; add practice time. | Intermittent stars or light: a shaky mind, not the light's fault; light does not disappear, the mind shakes. | Pain, numbness: ignore, hold attention at eye focus. | Attention drops to throat or heart: keep it at the eye centre.
- cautions: Never accept anything offered by a spirit within; Satan may appear as the Master, test by repeating the names. | Do not tell others of inner visions. | Do not meditate on sun, moon and stars as such; they are material; do not stop at them. | Photograph of the Master is only a reflection of one's own mind. | No attempt to locate the pineal gland or take the soul up by force.
- safety flags: transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: The sky, stars, sun, moon and the Jyoti are regions of the subtle plane on the way to the Master's radiant form; Shabd, not light, lifts the soul higher.
- practice: Sit in a posture you can hold for long. Close the eyes and repeat the five holy names mentally with the attention gathered at the eye centre between and behind the eyes, with no pressure on the eyes and breathing left alone. Do not try to look for light. Pain or numbness in the body below the eyes means the attention is withdrawing; ignore it. If you see only darkness, keep peeping into the darkness; it will change into light. Next you will see sparks and flashes, then a steady light, a starry sky and a big star. Fix on that star while repeating until it bursts and you pass through; do the same with the sun and moon. Lengthen sittings gradually, and tell no one what you see.
  - passage (onset; Letter 118, printed p. 190, OCR lines 8310-8340): If no light is seen, fix attention on the darkness and keep peeping into it; the darkness changes into light.
  - passage (stages; Printed p. 336-337, source lines 2385-2423): When concentration is nearly complete, sparks and fleeting flashes replace darkness, then the light steadies.
  - passage (caution; Source lines 1353-1389): Do not try to look for light; the faculty that sees is still outside; light is already inside and appears when we go in.
  - passage (method; Printed p. 72, source lines 556-617): Fix attention in the biggest star until it bursts and shows what is within and beyond; then cross sun and moon to the Master's form.
  - passage (caution; Source lines 2073-2135): Satan can appear as the Master offering powers; repeat the names to test; never ask for anything but the eternal home.

## [snt-tagore-songs-of-kabir] Songs of Kabir
Kabir (c. 1440-1518); 1915; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep_marginal; relevance 2, practicality 1, confidence medium (Poetry; translation by Tagore is free and selective.)
- mode: ambiguous. 'look within, and behold how the moon-beams ... shine in you' implies inner seeing, but the poems never separate perception from metaphor; 'The moon shines in my body, but my blind eyes cannot see it'.
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET unclear: No method for one who sees nothing: 'One day your eyes shall suddenly be opened'; the guru 'removes the veil from the eyes'. Implies grace/guru.
- techniques: devotion_or_transmission, sound_or_mantra; phenomena: moon_disc, sun_disc, lamp_or_flame, lightning_flashes, all_pervading_light
- conditions: gaze_or_eye_position: I. 76: eyes open, not shut; prerequisites: Guru
- with the light: Still the mind to silence before the splendour (I. 93).
- cautions: I. 76 rejects shutting eyes and ears and bodily mortification in favour of sahaj union with eyes open
- interpretation: The inner light of sun and moon is the presence of the Hidden One within the body.
- practice: Kabir gives no technique. He points within, where moonbeams of the Hidden One shine and the unstruck music sounds; before that splendour the mind is to be stilled. Opening of the inner eye is the guru's gift, sudden and unforced. In the sahaj song he refuses sense-closure altogether: he does not shut eyes or ears but sees with eyes open and beholds the Lord's beauty everywhere.
  - passage (mode; song at lines 975-978): Look within to see the moonbeams of the Hidden One.
  - passage (caution; I. 76 santo, sahaj samadh bhali, lines 1500-1502): Rejects closing the eyes and ears.
  - passage (onset; II. 33 ghar ghar dipak barai, lines 1113-1114): Seeing comes suddenly, not by effort described.

## [snt-tulsi-sahib-ghat-ramayan] Ghat Ramayan, Part 1 (Ghat Ramayan Bhag 1)
Tulsi Sahib of Hathras (c. 1763-1843); composed early 19th c.; this edition 1916; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 3, confidence medium (Read in original Hindi through noisy OCR with dropped vowel signs; the stage sequence is clear but individual words (e.g. the colour list) are partly conjectural.)
- mode: perceptual. Repeatedly 'seen' (दीसे, दिखावा, देखे, निरखि): a star 'appears' (दीसे इक तारा) at the sky-door; the light 'grows' as surat advances; the moon is 'seen in the ghat (body)'. Framed as seen with the inner eye (निज नैना) in the body, not as imagined.
- eyes: unspecified; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET induction: Instruction: in the black lotus the satguru dwells; know the til-sized point there; fix the mind there moment by moment so that not one blink breaks it (छिन छिन मन के तहाँ लगावे। एक पलक दुटन नहिं पावे), dwell in the til window night and day; then the star appears and the anahad sound is heard. Prerequisite: refuge at the satguru's feet (सतगरू पद). No time scale beyond 'night and day' and 'some days' (कछु दिवस) of travel within.
- techniques: gazing_fixed_point, concentration_nonvisual, sound_or_mantra, devotion_or_transmission; phenomena: stars, lightning_flashes, colour_fields, white, red, gold_yellow, green, lamp_or_flame, moon_disc, sun_disc, all_pervading_light, figures_or_forms
- conditions: gaze_or_eye_position: Surat fixed at the til (sesame-seed point) at the black lotus (स्याम कंज); duration_and_schedule: Unbroken, moment by moment; night and day; prerequisites: Satguru; other: Coupled with listening to the anahad nad
- stages: तिल / इक तारा: Surat fixed at the til; a single star appears at the sky-door; anahad nad heard -> बीज (lightning): Lightning flashes in the sky; light grows as surat moves toward the door -> पाँच रंग: White and black, then five colours: black, red, white, yellow, green -> जोति: Tej and jyoti shown, like a lamp seen through a temple door -> चंद: The jyoti bursts; a full moon seen within, moonlight spreads; many stars -> भान (sun): Star and moon fade; light like the midday sun in the ghat; the seven continents, oceans, rivers and all beings seen within
- with the light: Keep the surat steady (ठहराइ) and keep ascending; the lights mark successive stations, not the goal.
- obstacles/fixes: The mind must not break away even for a blink
- safety flags: transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: The lights are the stations of the surat's ascent through the inner sky (gagan) toward the satguru's abode.
- practice: Taking refuge in the satguru, fix the attention (surat) at the sesame-seed point in the dark lotus behind the eyes, renewing it moment by moment so that not even a blink breaks it, and live there night and day. A single star appears at the door of the sky and the unstruck sound is heard; listen and let the surat settle. Lightning flashes; the light grows as the surat advances. White and black appear, then five colours. Then a light like a lamp seen through a temple door; it bursts and a full moon appears within, then many stars; then a light like the midday sun fills the body, in which the whole cosmos is seen.
  - passage (onset; भक्ति का वरनन, file lines 3682-3686): Fix the mind on the til-sized point moment by moment, not breaking for a single blink; dwell in the til window night and day.
  - passage (stages; file lines 3686-3687): At the sky-door a single star appears; the unstruck sound is heard.
  - passage (stages; file lines 3693-3697): Lightning flashes in the sky; as the surat moves toward the door the light keeps growing; then five colours.
  - passage (stages; file lines 3710-3714): Jyoti appears like a lamp at a temple door; it bursts and a full moon is seen within.
  - passage (stages; file lines 3722-3723): Star and moon dim; a midday sun arises in the body.


# STREAM: Taoist inner alchemy and meditation (taoist)

## [dao-chia-awaken-healing-light] Awaken Healing Light of the Tao
Mantak Chia and Maneewan Chia; 1993; teacher_manual; rights in_copyright; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep_marginal; relevance 3, practicality 4, confidence high (Keyword pass over the whole text plus full reading of the relevant sections; organ-colour visualizations not read line by line but are plainly imaginal.)
- mode: ambiguous. Most light in the book is prescribed visualization ('picture', 'visualize', 'imagine' coloured lights and mists) with no claim it becomes seen. Two places mark seen light: in the Orbit FAQ, 'those who are visually oriented may see a light inside their heads, or points along the orbit may light up' as a sign that energy circulates; and the Higher Self light 'may be sensed with your inner eye as a benevolent golden cloud... fine particles or waves of light floating in space in front of you at eye level'. The sun/candle practice uses the afterimage ('lightly close the eyes and see the light in front of you'), which is stimulus-bound. Chia explicitly makes seeing optional: non-visual people 'may never see any colors with their inner eye' and feeling has 'the same end effect'.
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: no
- ONSET unclear: No method is offered to make light appear for someone who sees nothing; seeing is treated as a matter of temperament ('visually oriented'), not of attainment. To the person who feels or sees nothing the FAQ says: channels open at different rates; after two weeks without success reverse the flow one centre at a time, a week per centre; some students practise for years with no sensation and still benefit. Light in the head is just one possible sign of circulation among heat, tingling, bubbles, shaking. The nearest to induction is the external-light gathering (sun at sunrise/sunset, candle, 60-100 W bulb), whose afterimage is then 'drawn in', but it aims at absorbing 'solar essence' felt as Chi in the mouth, not at persistent seen light.
- techniques: light_source_gazing, afterimage_retention, eye_position, posture_or_body_lock, energy_work, visualization_crossing, breath_control; phenomena: light_in_head, sparks_or_points, gold_yellow, smoke_mist_cloud, violet, blue, white, lightning_flashes
- conditions: posture: seated on chair edge, feet flat; tongue to palate; gaze_or_eye_position: eyelids half closed, 'dim the irises'; eye spiralling exercises open and closed; inner eye turned to the Crystal Room/crown; setting: sunrise or sunset sun, candle, or 60-100 W bulb for light gathering; duration_and_schedule: light gathering 6-9 rounds; eye spirals 9-36 times; Orbit troubleshooting on a two-week then week-per-centre schedule; prerequisites: open both channels of the Microcosmic Orbit before dwelling on the Third Eye; other: contraction of iris, eye, mouth, anal and perineal sphincters in sync with inhalation
- stages: Orbit opening signs: Light in the head or points of the orbit lighting up, alongside heat, tingling, effervescence, shocks; taken as proof that Chi circulates, not as a stage to pursue.
- with the light: Condense it: draw seen or felt light into a dot or ball, bring it into the mideyebrow/mouth/Tan Tien and store it at the navel; never leave energy dwelling in the head.
- obstacles/fixes: No sensation or light: continue; after two weeks reverse the flow one centre at a time for a week each; benefit occurs without sensation | Cramp from forcing the light-gathering: exhale and relax into it; don't use force | Bright sun: blink 20-30 times and move the head lightly; use sunrise/sunset, candle or bulb
- cautions: Kundalini syndrome: focusing too long on the mideyebrow/Third Eye without opening the front (Functional) channel congests energy in the head, with headaches, ringing ears, seeing flashing lights, psychosis; always bring energy down to the navel | Sun gazing only near horizon, with blinking
- safety flags: sun_gazing
- interpretation: Light is Chi of specific frequencies (golden Higher Self, violet North Star, red Big Dipper, blue earth, white Po soul); seeing it is a temperament-dependent sign of circulating energy, equivalent to feeling it, and flashing lights in the head can be a pathology of unvented energy.
- practice: Chia does not teach seeing light as a goal. For light gathering: at sunrise or sunset (or with a candle or 60-100 W bulb), look at the light, inhale into the mideyebrow and mouth with eyelids half closed and irises 'dimmed', hold 10-15 seconds; exhale, open the eyes lightly and condense the 'light Chi' in the saliva. Add a light smile, contraction of eye, mouth, anal and perineal sphincters, and the tongue on the palate; 6-9 rounds; swallow the charged saliva. With bright sun, blink 20-30 times, close the eyes and draw in the light seen in front of you. In the Microcosmic Orbit, light in the head or at orbit points may appear for visually oriented people as one sign of circulation; whether it appears or not, condense energy into dots and store it at the navel. Do not dwell on the Third Eye before the front channel is open.
  - passage (mode; Ch. XV, FAQ 'How will I know when my Orbit is open?', source lines 18629-18632): Visually oriented practitioners may see light inside the head or points on the orbit light up; any such sensation shows energy is circulating.
  - passage (onset; Intro, colours of energies, source lines 882-885): Some people are not visually oriented and may never see colours with the inner eye; they feel the frequencies instead, with the same result.
  - passage (onset; Ch. XV, FAQ 'I don't feel anything', source lines 18634-18647): If after two weeks nothing is felt, reverse the flow centre by centre, a week each; some students practise for years without sensations and still benefit.
  - passage (method; Ch. IV, 'Condensing light into the body', source lines 5464-5547): Gaze at sun near horizon, candle or bulb; with bright sun blink 20-30 times, close the eyes, see the light before you, and draw it in with dimmed eyes and sphincter contractions, condensing it in the saliva.
  - passage (caution; Ch. XV, Kundalini syndrome, source lines 19284-19293): Focusing too long on the Third Eye without the front channel open can cause energy congestion with headaches, ringing ears, flashing lights and psychosis.
- new leads: Lee Sannella, The Kundalini Syndrome (cited by Chia for light-related head congestion)

## [dao-dacheng-jieyao] 大成捷要 (Dacheng jieyao): 'yang light appears three times' and other light signs by stage
Anonymous (Wu-Liu / Longmen manuscript manual; sections attributed to Qiu Chuji, Liu Huayang and others); late Qing manuscript; first lithographed with preface dated December 1929; teacher_manual; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 3, confidence high (Relevant sections read in full in the original. Light here is a timing sign within a celibate sexual-alchemy course, not a goal; section attributions to Bodhidharma etc. are the manual's own.)
- mode: perceptual. The yang light is located and described as seen: 'like lightning drawn in cloud, like the empty room producing white (云中掣电、虚室生白), first appearing before the brows'; at the second appearance light 'surges up from the abdomen to the eyes and shines, forcing both eyes open (直将二目催开)'. 九灵老人 insists these are 'not illusions, but real tests produced by true work' and 'not metaphor but real scenes (非比喻也，乃真景象也)'.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET development_only: Nothing is offered for inducing light as such. The light is a by-product and timing sign of the Wu-Liu hundred-day course: inner gaze/'returning the light' (回光返照, 塞兑垂帘) into the qi cavity, small-orbit firing on each 'living zi hour' erection, ~300 orbits. The first flash comes during refining of essence 'while fire is not yet sufficient'; the second only after genital retraction (马阴藏相/龟缩不举) and full 300 orbits; the third 'after a day or at the opposite hour' of stillness. Light seen before the bodily signs 'is certainly not the sprout of the great medicine, mostly arising from deluded thought (多属妄想而发)'. Prerequisites: celibacy, stopping leakage of essence; the text claims oral transmission (口口传).
- techniques: concentration_nonvisual, eye_position, breath_control, energy_work, eye_pressure_or_sense_closure, posture_or_body_lock, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena, visualization_crossing; phenomena: lightning_flashes, white, gold_yellow, sphere_pearl_bindu, red, body_or_objects_luminous, all_pervading_light, light_in_the_room, smoke_mist_cloud, violet
- conditions: posture: seated meditation; a wooden seat pressing the perineum, nose clip, tongue to palate during the seven-day gathering; gaze_or_eye_position: eyes lowered like a curtain (垂帘), 'eye-light' (眸光/二目阳光) fixed on the lower dantian/qi cavity; setting: quiet room; for gestation, secluded, away from noise; duration_and_schedule: practice at the zi hour after midnight when yang stirs; ~300 small orbits (the 'hundred days'); seven days and nights of unbroken gazing for the great medicine; ten months gestation; prerequisites: celibacy and cessation of seminal leakage; calming of mind first (炼心为主); other: Women's method (late section) uses breasts and 'returning the light to the life palace'.
- stages: 阳光一现 (first appearance): Lightning-like white flash between the brows during refining essence; fire not yet sufficient, continue firing orbits. -> 阳光二现 (second appearance): After genital retraction and 300 orbits, light surges from the abdomen to the eyes, 2-3 flashes, sometimes like a gold coin or red fire-pearl dripping; signal to stop the orbit fire and sit in stillness 'containing the light'. -> 阳光三现 (third appearance): After a day or twelve hours, the same light again flashes between the brows from the dantian: 'moonlight bright on the south-west road'; stop fire at once and gather the great medicine over seven days. A fourth appearance means the medicine is lost. -> 道身证验 / 胎中景: Later: red haze over crown, lightning in the eyes, organs seen within 'as by candlelight', body like a crystal pagoda, golden light enveloping body; after union of sun and moon a wheel-sized auspicious light from the crown, bright day and night; after gestation 'nature-light', purple mist and a ball of golden light over the body.
- with the light: Use as a timing sign only; at the second appearance stop circulating and 'hold the light' in stillness (含光静养); at the later visions, do not look at it (不可看他), 'kill the mind' and enter samadhi; ignore all apparitions.
- obstacles/fixes: Light appearing without genital retraction: false, from deluded thought and insufficient fire; keep firing. | Continuing the fire past the third appearance (a fourth appearance) loses the medicine. | During gestation, visions of water (黄石公) mean dantian fire has gone cold and dan-light has vanished; fix by gaze-fixing and visualizing a blazing true fire filling the cauldron with spirit-light. | Uncontrollable visions of fire/flames from the seven orifices (李虚庵/尹大真人): visualize a cold black ball the size of a cartwheel in the space before you and draw it into the belly, repeating until the false fire subsides.
- cautions: Light without the bodily sign is 妄想 and not a true sign. | All visions (heavens, hells, demons, thunder-gods) are to be met with a dead-still mind; the 'thunder god' is the discursive mind (识神) trying to frighten you. | Stopping fire too early or too late both ruin the work.
- safety flags: breath_retention, transmission_required_by_tradition, tradition_warns_against_seeking, fasting
- interpretation: The light is the dan's own radiance (丹光/阳光), the outward sign that pre-heaven yang qi has fully gathered in the qi root; true only when accompanied by the bodily sign of retraction.
- practice: Calm the mind first; sit, lower the eyelids, close the senses and turn the gaze inward onto the lower dantian (回光返照). Each time yang stirs after midnight, run a small heavenly circuit with breath and intent, and keep a count of good circuits toward 300. A white, lightning-like flash between the brows during this phase is the first appearance: it means the fire is not yet sufficient; keep going. Only when the genitals retract and stay unaroused, and light surges from the belly to the eyes, flashing two or three times, has the second appearance come: stop the circulating fire, sit in deep stillness and hold the light without effort. Within a day or so the light flashes a third time between the brows; then begin the seven-day gathering with the eye-light fixed unbroken on the qi cavity. Light that comes without the bodily sign is delusion. In later stages, whatever luminous visions arise, do not look at them; stay in samadhi.
  - passage (onset; 真阳发生天机 (达摩祖师曰), source line 496): Even if outer light appears [before true retraction], it is certainly not the sprout of the great medicine; it mostly arises from deluded thought and the fire is not yet sufficient.
  - passage (mode; 真阳发生天机, source line 496): The dan emits rays of light, like lightning drawn through clouds, like an empty room producing whiteness; first seen before the brows, later rising from the lower field to the eyes, flashing bright: this is the first appearance.
  - passage (stages; 真阳发生天机, source line 496): At the second appearance, in stillness, dan-light suddenly surges up in the abdomen to the eyes and shines, forcing the eyes open, flashing two or three times, sometimes bright as a gold coin or red as a fire-pearl; do not run the orbit fire again; enter samadhi and nourish the light.
  - passage (development; 阳光三现天机 (华阳禅师曰), source line 503): After stopping the fire, never leave the fire: constantly warm and nourish, moment by moment concentrate the gaze, to await the third appearance, which comes after a day or twelve hours; stop at once then, or a fourth appearance loses the medicine.
  - passage (testimony; 道身证验说 (九灵老人曰), source line 168): Among verifications: seeing a needle through a wall at night, red haze circling the crown, lightning in the eyes, inner viewing of the organs bright as by candlelight, the body like a crystal pagoda covered in golden light; insisted to be real, not illusion.
  - passage (caution; 火焚禅坐危险详说 (尹大真人云), source line 788): Remedy for runaway fire-light: visualize in the space before you a black ball as big as a cartwheel, ice-cold, draw it into the belly; repeat three to five times until the false fire is gone.

## [dao-huiming-jing-chinese] 慧命經 (Huiming jing; Hui Ming Ching, 'Book of Consciousness and Life'), Chinese text
Liu Huayang 柳華陽; 1794; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 3, confidence high (Relevant chapters read in the original; 決疑 Q&A only partly checked.)
- mode: perceptual. Signs are stated as seen: 'golden light in the eyes' (眼有金光) among the six quakings; 'often in a dark room one sees white light' (常於暗實之中，或見白光); before the relic leaves the furnace 'from dantian to the eyes all is empty white dazzling, bright as moonlight'; at leaving concentration white or golden light, snowflakes, a golden disc like a cart wheel. Liu also says light appearing without the bodily signs is 'born of imagination', not relic-light.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET development_only: The light is not a target of induction: the entry method is to gather spirit into the lower dantian (凝神返照龍宮) with breath as 'wind' blowing the 'fire' of intent, refining sexual essence; the seen white/golden light is a sign that the relic (舍利) is complete, used as a timing cue for gathering. Time scale for first stirring: 'youth not more than a month, middle age three to five months'; relic gathering in 5-7 days of continuous fixed gaze on it. Repeatedly insists a true teacher is needed, especially at the light of leaving concentration. Light without genital retraction (馬陰藏) is imagination.
- techniques: energy_work, breath_control, concentration_nonvisual, devotion_or_transmission; phenomena: white, gold_yellow, moon_disc, sun_disc, sparks_or_points, all_pervading_light
- conditions: posture: sitting; 'walking, standing, sitting, lying' not leaving this place; gaze_or_eye_position: spirit-light 'turned back' (迴光返照) into the dantian / 'northern sea'; during gathering the eyes fix on the relic (專視); setting: not necessarily seclusion; mocks withdrawal to mountains; duration_and_schedule: day and night without break; 7-day gathering; prerequisites: male practitioner, retention of essence; true transmission; other: counts of breath cycles (36/24) for the 'dharma wheel' orbit
- stages: 真種產 true seed: warm blissful stirring in stillness -> 舍利成 relic complete: 虛室生白, dantian hot as boiling water, genitals retract -> 六種震動: golden light in the eyes, wind in ears, etc.; white light seen in a dark room, counted -> 出爐: from dantian to eyes all empty-white dazzling like moonlight -> 出定: white or golden light, snowflakes filling space; then a golden disc like a cart wheel appears in space, to be drawn into the dharma body
- with the light: Use the white light's appearances as the cue to gather the relic; at leaving concentration gather the light back in (收光) or it disperses; do not recognise buddhas or bodhisattvas appearing; wait for the golden wheel and absorb it.
- obstacles/fixes: Residual 'leak shadows' (霞影) stir desire: refine with breath-wind until the yin demon becomes yang light (陰魔化為陽光) | Too much martial fire at relic completion scatters it: switch to the civil fire of spirit-light turned back
- cautions: Light without the bodily signs is imagination, not relic-light | Visions of buddhas/bodhisattvas on leaving concentration are demonic transformations; do not converse with them | Teacher required to gather the light at leaving concentration; mocks dry Chan sitting
- safety flags: transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: The seen light is the outward sign of refined vital essence (慧命, 舍利) uniting with nature; nature-light (性光) alone, cultivated by mind without life, is yin spirit and incomplete.
- practice: This is a manual of internal alchemy, not a light practice. Gather spirit and turn its light back into the lower belly (the 'dragon palace'), holding intent and breath together there, forgetting and illuminating at once, neither forcing nor neglecting. Use the breath as wind to 'blow' the fire of intent; at sexual arousal, settle it with mind and breath until it retracts. When warmth and stirring arise, circulate them along the 'dharma wheel' with counted breaths. Light appears as a sign: when the relic is complete, the 'empty room gives birth to white', the belly is hot, the genitals retract; golden light in the eyes and white light seen in the dark tell when to gather; fix the gaze on it continuously for up to seven days. Treat light without those bodily signs as imagination. Later, on leaving deep concentration, white or golden light and 'snow' appear: do not engage visions; draw the golden disc back in, ideally under a teacher.
  - passage (stages; 集說慧命經第九, comment on 火化以後收取舍利, source line 282): When the relic is complete the empty room produces white, the dantian is like boiling water and the genitals retract.
  - passage (mode; 集說慧命經第九, comment on 眉間常放白毫光, source line 318): Often in a dark room one sees white light; its appearances are the gathering cue; before the relic leaves the furnace all is white and dazzling from dantian to eyes like moonlight; light without the bodily sign is imagination.
  - passage (testimony; 集說慧命經第九, source line 314): Six quakings: golden light in the eyes, wind sound in ears, twitching in nose, eagle cry behind the head, body leaping, fire-pearl racing in dantian.
  - passage (method; 集說慧命經第九, source line 190): In gathering spirit, no thought out or in, empty and unattached, light turned back nearby; forgetting and illumination are one.
  - passage (caution; 集說慧命經第九, comment on 從白毫光出, source line 410): At leaving concentration white or golden light is emitted; seek a teacher for the method of gathering the light, or it scatters.
  - passage (development; 正道修煉直論第十, source line 518): Long refining with breath turns the yin demon of desire into yang light.

## [dao-jinhua-zongzhi-chinese] 太乙金華宗旨 (Taiyi jinhua zongzhi; The Secret of the Golden Flower), Chinese text
Attributed to Lü Dongbin (spirit-writing text, Qing dynasty); late 17th to 18th century; Daozang jiyao recension reprinted 1906; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 5, confidence high (Full Chinese text read; wikisource transcription has some typos (曰 for 日 etc.).)
- mode: perceptual. Ch.6 sign: in stillness 'the light of the eyes surges, all before one is white, as if in cloud' (目光騰騰，滿前皆白，如在雲中), and on opening the eyes the body cannot be found; ch.3 'the light is only real after a hundred days' and ch.9 distinguishes mere 'eye-light' (目光) from true light, implying a seen phenomenon. But ch.10 makes the seen light secondary: grasped as light it becomes consciousness-light.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: For someone who sees nothing: lower the lids 'like a curtain' to the point where the nose-tip is just visible, fix that once as a plumb-line and let go; rest attention level between the eyebrows and 'the light enters of itself' (光自然透入), no need to project it. Pair with listening to the breath until it is inaudible. 'Do not seek signs; signs come of themselves' (不求驗而驗自至). Time scales: 100 days of concentrated work before 'the light is real' and becomes spirit-fire; ch.7 'two or three months' of daily practice; a grain of true yang (黍珠) appears in the light after 100 days. Ch.10: before light is seen, that (not-seeing) is itself the effect; if one fixes on light as light it falls into consciousness; 'do not care whether there is light or not'. Ch.9: a true teacher's confirmation is needed; intellectual effort makes it heterodox.
- techniques: eye_position, concentration_nonvisual, breath_control, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena, energy_work; phenomena: white, smoke_mist_cloud, all_pervading_light, moon_disc, sun_disc, sphere_pearl_bindu
- conditions: posture: upright seated (正身安坐); gaze_or_eye_position: lids half-lowered (垂簾) so the nose tip is just seen; then attention between the brows; later the two eyes 'shine inward' on the Kan palace (lower belly); setting: quiet sitting; also carried into daily activity as 返照; duration_and_schedule: early morning one incense-stick or 1-2 hours; uninterrupted daily; 100 days foundation; prerequisites: ethical everyday life (人倫日用); calm and intelligence; other: if drowsy, get up and walk, then sit again
- stages: 金華乍吐 (flower first opens): continuous stillness, joy, as if drunk or bathed; warmth through the body -> 金華正放 (flower fully open): bright moon in mid-sky, the whole earth felt as a realm of light -> 金華大凝 (great congealing): body full and strong; further marvels -> 現在證驗 present signs: sounds as from a valley; eye-light surging, all white as in cloud, body not findable on opening the eyes (虛室生白); body light and rising -> 百日後 黍珠: after 100 days a point of true yang, a millet-pearl, arises within the light; wait quietly
- with the light: Do not grasp it as light or apply intention (that is consciousness-light); keep turning it back; after 100 days wait quietly for the pearl; in ch.8 guide it with the eyes up to the crown and back down to the yellow court.
- obstacles/fixes: Drowsiness (昏沉): attend to fine breath, listen to its soundlessness; if sleepy, get up and walk | Scatter (散亂): knowing it is already its cure; use breath as handle | Eyes too open: scatter outward; too closed: sinking; half-lowered is the mean | Do not keep staring at the nose tip; set it like a mason's line and let go | Thoughts: investigate where a thought arises and ceases (止觀 alternation)
- cautions: Ch.5: do not sit in blank emptiness (無記空) | do not force with will and over-seriousness; the true signal is between present and absent | do not fall into the realm of the skandhas: cold, sinking breath, 'dead wood and cold ash', ends as wood and stone | do not follow arising trains of images; that leads to the desire realm, rebirth as fox-spirit | Ch.10: light seized with intent is consciousness-light, not nature-light | Ch.12: no method of 'gathering' the medicine-light; it appears only after great stillness
- safety flags: tradition_warns_against_seeking, transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: The light is the original spirit/nature (性光), the 'golden flower', normally leaking out through the eyes; turning it back is concentration-insight (止觀), and its seen forms are confirmations, not the goal.
- practice: Sit upright in a quiet place, ideally early morning, for an incense-stick or longer, every day without a break. Lower the eyelids like a curtain until the tip of the nose is just visible; use this once to set the gaze, then let it go. Rest attention lightly, level between the eyebrows, without pushing it inward; the text says the light enters of itself. Join this with the breath: listen to it until it is too fine to hear, and let it grow finer. When a thought arises, look into where it comes from and where it goes, then return to resting (止 and 觀 in turn). If drowsy, get up and walk. Avoid blank vacancy, effortful straining, cold heavy sinking, and drifting with image streams. Do not care whether there is light or not; if light appears and you fix on it as light, it becomes consciousness-light. Signs like whiteness filling the field 'as in a cloud' are confirmations. Expect about a hundred days of steady work before the light is 'real'.
  - passage (method; 第三章 回光守中, source lines 76): Only half-lowered lids hit the mean, just seeing the nose tip; that is taken as the standard, then the light enters of itself without your projecting it.
  - passage (onset; 第三章, source line 80): Fix attention level between the brows; light is a lively thing and enters of itself.
  - passage (stages; 第三章, source line 63): After a hundred days of concentrated work the light becomes real and is spirit-fire; then a millet pearl of true yang appears in it.
  - passage (mode; 第六章 回光證驗, source line 130): In stillness eye-light surges, all before one is white as in cloud; opening the eyes, one cannot find the body: the empty room produces white.
  - passage (caution; 第五章 回光差謬, source lines 113-118): Errors: blank emptiness, forced will, falling into the skandha-realm of cold sinking, and following trains of arising images.
  - passage (caution; 第十章 性光識光, source line 190): Before light is seen, that is the sign; if seen as light and seized with intent, it falls into consciousness and is not nature-light. Do not care whether there is light or not.
- new leads: DZJY recension (sources/dao-jinhua-zongzhi-chinese-dzjy.txt) not compared for variants

## [dao-taiping-shengjun-mizhi] 太平經聖君祕旨 (Taiping jing shengjun mizhi): the 'method of guarding the light of the One' (守一明法)
Anonymous; transmitted to Shangxiang Qingtong jun (digest of Taiping jing material); Tang-period compilation of Han-to-Six-Dynasties material (Kanripo catalogue: 唐); classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 4, confidence high (Whole text visible; terse classical Chinese with ambiguous words (表).)
- mode: perceptual. "With eyes shut there is no light in the eyes"; then light arises and "one sees clearly the four directions"; colours and intensities graded; light described as firefly then lightning.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET induction: Explicitly addresses the beginner who sees darkness: before refinement the closed eyes are dark; with long guarding the light arises of itself. Method is to guard the One (unify essence, spirit, qi) in a quiet thick-walled room, with little food, no joy or anger, no clever effort, "everything arises gradually", about a hundred days for first signs; 100/200/300 days as small/middle/great stillness. Dark both outside and inside with nothing seen is an illness, to be helped with medicines.
- techniques: concentration_nonvisual, sensory_restriction, fasting_or_austerity, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena, energy_work; phenomena: white, red, blue, gold_yellow, black_light, fireflies, lightning_flashes, light_in_the_room, body_or_objects_luminous
- conditions: posture: Sit upright; later sit or lie quietly; setting: Quiet room with thick walls, no noise; duration_and_schedule: Gradual; about 100 days to first effect; 100, 200, 300 days stillness grades; prerequisites: Moral conduct, no joy or anger, little food, avoid clever effort; other: Imagine the body hollow and white as jade; unite essence, spirit and qi
- stages: dark: Closed eyes show nothing. -> light arises: Starts red as a new fire, long guarding turns it pure white, then penetratingly bright to the distance; first like fireflies then lightning. -> graded lights: Green/blue: lesser yang; red like fire: for transcending the world; yellow-blue: harmonious; white like clear water: lesser yin; dark-clear like peering at water: great yin; dark outside but belly lit within: great harmony.
- with the light: Guard it, do not lose it; it enables seeing the body and the spirits, leading to transformation.
- obstacles/fixes: Dark inside and out: disorder, treat with medicine and seek the earlier seven methods | Excess clever intent: let the One come of itself
- cautions: Visions of five-organ spirits and others beyond the first two methods are mixed or false; reject them | Emotion, noise, heavy food spoil the guarding
- safety flags: tradition_warns_against_seeking
- interpretation: The One is the primal source; its light is the illumination of essence-spirit-qi united, with the body becoming luminous.
- practice: Prepare a quiet room with thick walls, eat little, and keep calm: no joy, anger or cleverness. Unite essence, spirit and qi into one by simply keeping the body in mind as hollow, white, and jade-like. At first the closed eyes see only dark; this is normal. Persist gradually for around a hundred days, and light arises of itself: first red like a fresh flame, then white, then bright to the distance, like fireflies at first and later lightning. Do not contrive. Rejecting visions of spirits or ancestors, keep the guard. Complete darkness within and without for a long time is a disorder needing medicine.
  - passage (onset; opening, Part A lines 45-47): Before the method is refined, closed eyes are dark with no light in them.
  - passage (onset; Part A lines 47-48): Continued guarding makes light arise on its own and the four directions become visible.
  - passage (stages; Part A lines 50-53): The light begins red and with long practice becomes pure white.
  - passage (stages; Part A lines 149-151): Light grows from a firefly to lightning.
  - passage (caution; Part A line 65): Dark outside and inside with nothing seen counts as disorder, to be aided with medicines.

## [dao-wu-shouyang-xianfo-hezong] 仙佛合宗語錄 (Xianfo hezong yulu): 'the yang light appears' as the sign for stopping the fire
Wu Shouyang 伍守陽 (Wu Chongxu 伍沖虛); first half of the 17th century; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 3, confidence high (Wikisource recension; candidate notes that the Daozang jiyao recension lacks the second/third-appearance wording. Dacheng jieyao's 真阳发生天机 closely paraphrases Wu's ch.4.)
- mode: perceptual. The light is located and seen: 'the space between the brows, called Bright Hall, is where the yang light appears; when it appears it is like a flash of lightning, the empty room producing white (恍如掣電，虛室生白)'; 'in stillness one suddenly sees lightning again between the brows'. Signs of the medicine include 'the eyes emit golden light (眼吐金光)'.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET development_only: No induction of light per se: it is the outward sign of the completed small-orbit firing (300 'mysterious' circuits) plus genital retraction. The first flash comes during refining essence while 'the fire is not complete'; the second and third come only in stillness after the 300 count. Wu stresses 'when the medicine is not ripe the fire has no stopping-sign (丹藥未成熟，則火無止景)', i.e. light cannot be hurried; one practitioner reached fire-sufficiency in under fifty days and the medicine in five. The method of gathering is the 'eye-light' (眸光) gazing fixedly; Qiu Chuji's verse: 'the whole of life is in the two pupils'. Presupposes teacher transmission and celibacy.
- techniques: concentration_nonvisual, eye_position, breath_control, energy_work, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena; phenomena: lightning_flashes, white, gold_yellow, sphere_pearl_bindu, snow_or_flakes
- conditions: posture: seated meditation; gaze_or_eye_position: eye-light (眸光) fixed on the centre/lower field to give spirit its 'stabilizing point'; duration_and_schedule: 300 good circuits of small orbit; seven-day gathering of the great medicine; ten months gestation; prerequisites: pure 'water source' (no lustful thought), proper gathering and sealing, no dullness or scatter; transmission from a teacher
- stages: 陽光一現: Lightning-white flash between the brows during refining essence; continue firing. -> 陽光二現: After 300 circuits, in samadhi, lightning again between the brows, with retraction: stop the fire (止火之景). -> 陽光三現: A third flash: the great medicine can be gathered; a fourth means overfiring and loss. -> 得藥六景: During the seven-day gathering, around days 3-4: fire in the dantian, kidneys like boiling, eyes emitting golden light, wind behind the ears, eagle cries behind the head; 'golden light under the eyes' is the sign yang has become full. -> 出神景: At the end of nurturing, in samadhi one sees 'six-petalled flakes falling in the void' (snow): the sign to send out the yang spirit.
- with the light: Treat as a sign: at the second appearance stop running the fire even if qi stirs, enter samadhi and 'listen quietly' for the third; then gather.
- obstacles/fixes: Mistaking qi stirrings after the second appearance for something to gather and resuming the fire causes danger. | Stopping fire with no stopping-sign leaves qi insufficient; never stopping it leaves only longevity, not transcendence. | Bad circuits (dull, scattered, impure) do not count toward the 300.
- cautions: Firing to a fourth appearance turns the medicine back into post-heaven essence. | Wu criticizes reducing 回光內照 to merely 'turning the gaze inward': it must accompany each stage's object (essence-qi, spirit-qi, emptiness).
- safety flags: transmission_required_by_tradition, tradition_warns_against_seeking
- interpretation: The yang light is the visible proof that yang qi has fully settled in the qi root; Wu equates it with Buddhist images like the Buddha 'emitting light and shaking the earth'.
- practice: Within the Wu-Liu course: refine essence by running the small heavenly circuit whenever yang stirs, counting only circuits done with a pure mind, unbroken attention, and harmonised spirit and qi, toward three hundred. A lightning-like white flash between the brows in this phase is the first appearance and means continue. When the count is full, stop doing anything and sit in stillness, nourishing yang. When the flash comes again between the brows, with the genitals retracted and unaroused, that is the second appearance: stop the fire completely even if qi still stirs. Wait quietly for the third flash, then gather the great medicine for seven days by fixing the eye-light unwaveringly, letting breath settle until it stops by itself. Golden light in the eyes, wind behind the ears and cries behind the head mark the medicine's arrival. Do not overrun into a fourth appearance.
  - passage (mode; 火足候止火景采大藥候天機第四, source line 75): Between the brows, called the Bright Hall, is where the yang light appears; it appears like a flash of lightning, the empty room producing white.
  - passage (stages; 第四章, source line 83): So at the second appearance, even if there is movement, remove the fire and enter samadhi to nourish true yang, quietly awaiting the third, which again flashes between the brows; second and third are both stopping-signs, the third alone also the sign to gather.
  - passage (onset; 止火 Q&A, source line 1063): While the dan medicine is not ripe, the fire shows no stopping-sign: the light cannot be produced before the work is complete.
  - passage (method; 七日采大藥天機第五, source line 117): All four methods use the eye-light as what attracts and gathers; with the gaze fixed, around days three to four the six signs appear, including the eyes emitting golden light.
  - passage (stages; 出神景出神收神法第八, source line 165): When nurturing is complete, one suddenly sees in samadhi six-petalled flakes falling thickly in the void: the sign for sending out the spirit.

## [dao-zhengao-j9-10] 真誥 (Zhen'gao, Declarations of the Perfected), juan 9-10: inner viewing, eye methods, 'ingesting mist', light of sun and moon
Yang Xi (revelations, 364-370) and others; compiled and annotated by Tao Hongjing 陶弘景; revelations 4th century; compiled c. 499; classical_text; rights public_domain; access partial_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 4, confidence high (Both juan read whole in the original, unpunctuated; punctuation and sense divisions in the paraphrases are mine. The phrase 覺令見光分明 is compressed and its exact force (see light on pressing, or see light clearly afterwards) cannot be settled from this text alone. Whether the penetrating sight promised after a year is self-luminous inner light or sharpened ordinary vision is not stated. Sun and moon methods in other juan of the Zhen'gao were not available.)
- mode: imaginal_crossing. The verb throughout is 存 (hold in mind), but several items say the practice is to end in seeing or feeling. Ingesting mist: with half-closed eyes (臨目) 'make the eyes see' (使目見) five-coloured vapours winding thickly over the face. Five-star method: 'the essential of visualising is that you should feel your eyes behold (當覺目睹) the five stars'. Inner viewing of the viscera: 'practised long, it of itself becomes distinct and clear (自得分明了了)'. One item is plainly perceptual and mechanical: at the end of the eye massage, press the four corners of the eyes eighteen times and 'feel, and make yourself see, the light distinctly (覺令見光分明)'. Against this, most sun-and-moon items stay at 存 with only promised results (keen sight, a glowing face), and one late oral formula asks only that the image be 'as if a semblance' (使精如彷彿).
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: unclear
- ONSET induction: Addresses the beginner directly. (1) The inner-viewing formula says outright that at first there is truly not even a semblance, and that only long practice brings one into the marvel; it also advises starting with one direction rather than all four. (2) It gives a physical induction: pressing the corners of the eyes (with breath held, in one version) until light is seen. (3) Time scales are stated: about one year for sharpened sight and hearing or being able to write at night; 'long practice' for seeing a thousand li and seeing spirits; five years for the body to have radiance in the sun-in-heart method; Jiwei fasted three years before he 'got it' and divine light shone on his body. Prerequisites: curing illness first (Wang Zhongfu inhaled the two luminaries forty years with no result until he treated his illness), sexual restraint, purification fasts, a metal-pledge oath for the Baoshen eye method. No statement that light comes only by grace; the stress is on diligence (懃) and concentrated sincerity (精心), though 'resonance' (感) with the spirits is the stated mechanism.
- techniques: eye_pressure_or_sense_closure, visualization_crossing, breath_control, sound_or_mantra, light_source_gazing, sleep_threshold, energy_work, fasting_or_austerity, other; phenomena: light_on_eye_pressure, colour_fields, smoke_mist_cloud, violet, blue, red, sun_disc, moon_disc, rays, lamp_or_flame, body_or_objects_luminous, figures_or_forms, inner_sounds_of_metal_jade_strings_flutes, distant_scenes
- conditions: posture: Lying or sitting 'as you please' for most methods; the inner-viewing method is done lying still with faint breath so that bystanders think you asleep; the night eye method is done lying facing east (sitting also allowed).; gaze_or_eye_position: Eyes shut (閉目) for inner viewing; half-closed, lowered eyes (臨目) for the mist method, the sun-in-heart method and the three-vapour method (the last optionally while looking at the rising sun).; setting: Bedchamber or quiet room (寢靜); done privately and unseen; bed raised three feet or more; room and clothing kept clean.; duration_and_schedule: Dawn (平旦) for mist, three vapours and guarding the dark and white (from dawn until near noon); on waking in the night for eye massage and incantation; midnight on the 5th, 15th, 25th, 29th for the sun-in-heart method, or three times daily; eye-point pressing morning, noon and evening.; prerequisites: Illness treated first; sexual abstinence; no meat of the six domestic animals or pungent vegetables (for guarding the dark and white); purification fast and abstention from grain (for the Mingtang sun and moon method); oath with gold rings for the Baoshen and Siming eye methods; secrecy.; other: Nearly every method closes with saliva swallowing (咽液), teeth knocking (叩齒) and a whispered incantation (微祝). Tao Hongjing notes the detailed sequences are in his Dengzhen yinjue (登眞隱訣), not reproduced here.
- stages: 初爲之實無彷彿 (at first truly no semblance): Nothing is seen when inner viewing is first attempted; the instruction is to keep on, one direction at a time. -> 一年許耳目便精明 (after about a year): Eyes and ears become keen; the Baoshen massage says one can write at night after a year. -> 久爲之 (long practice): Sight 'penetrates a thousand li', spirits are seen (得見百靈, 羅映神靈), and sounds of metal, jade, strings and flutes are constantly heard in the ears. -> 神光映身 / 身有光彩 (light on the body): Sun and moon methods promise jade light on the face and golden sheen on the body, radiance of the body after five years, and report 'divine light shining on the body' for Jiwei after a three-year fast.
- with the light: Draw it in and swallow it: the five-coloured vapours, the rays of sun, moon and stars are taken into the mouth and swallowed with saliva; the three vapours are let turn to fire that burns round the body until the viscera are lit through; sun and moon are 'parked' in the Mingtang behind the brows, left and right, to merge with the pupils when not practising. The eye-pressure light is simply to be seen distinctly as a check on the 'eye spirits'.
- obstacles/fixes: Nothing appears at first: begin with one direction only, keep on; 'after a long time you really enter the marvel'. | Years of luminary practice without effect (Wang Zhongfu, forty years): the body was ill and the 'brain palace' depleted; treat illness and take medicines first, then practise. | Dim eyes and blocked ears come from within, from lust and from much worry: 'wash the heart, cut off thoughts, give up wandering lust' before the eye method. | Beginners start with zeal and then slacken, like a cart whose joints loosen: pursue the Way as a merchant pursues profit. | Fear at night or in the dark: visualise sun and moon returning into the Mingtang; evil influences vanish in a moment.
- cautions: Do not tell others what you see or hear, nor that you are practising; speaking of it invites trial by spirits and loss of merit. | Guarding the dark and white: sexual intercourse while practising 'is death'; also forbids meat and pungent foods. | Deafness in practitioners is blamed on practising the 'yellow and red' (sexual) qi without measure. | The Baoshen and Siming eye methods are transmitted only under an oath pledged with gold (and blue silk). | The order of teeth-knocking, incantation and swallowing must not be muddled.
- safety flags: eye_pressure, breath_retention, sun_gazing, fasting, transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: The eyes and ears are 'the ladder to the Perfected and the gate to the numinous'; light seen is the radiance of the body's own eye spirits (眼神, 目童) fed by the essences of sun, moon, stars and coloured vapours, and its brightening is both a sign of rejuvenation and the means of seeing spirits.
- practice: On waking in the night or at dawn, in a clean private room, lie or sit. Rub the palms hot and wipe the eyes; press the small hollows behind the eyebrows 27 times, rub the cheekbones under the eyes, rotate the ears 30 times, stroke the forehead upward from between the brows 27 times while holding in mind an infant with sun and moon shining as a pair, swallowing saliva throughout. Finish by pressing the four corners of the eyes 18 times until light is seen distinctly. A variant presses only the inner corners while holding the breath, releasing when breath must pass, then repeating. For inner viewing, lie still, eyes shut, breath faint, and send sight and hearing far off in one direction; expect nothing at first. At dawn, eyes shut, look within as if seeing the viscera, exhale 24 times with eyes half closed, making the eyes see five-coloured vapours coiling over the face; inhale them 50 times and swallow. Continue daily; results are counted in years.
  - passage (onset; Juan 9, item citing 紫度炎光内視中方, base-edition pp. 005a-005b (Part A lines 108-116)): Lie with eyes shut, body settled and breath faint, as if asleep so that bystanders do not notice; then look inward and listen afar to the four quarters, fixing ears and eyes beyond ten thousand li. Practised long, one of oneself sees things ten thousand li away, and with concentrated mind a million li; in the ears one constantly hears tones of metal and jade, strings and flutes. 'Four quarters' is a general expression: begin with one direction and fix inner sight and hearing there. When you first do it there is truly not even a semblance; after a long time you really do enter the marvel.
  - passage (method; Juan 9, 清靈眞人說寳神經, close of the eye-massage sequence, base-edition p. 007b (Part A lines 158-159); the related breath-held version from 太上天關三經 is at p. 005b (lines 117-119)): When the whole massage is done, press the four corners of the eyes with the hands eighteen times, feeling and making yourself see the light distinctly: this is the way of inspecting the eye spirits; done for a long time one comes to see the hundred numinous beings. Tao Hongjing's note says the exact measures are set out separately in his Dengzhen yinjue. The Tianguan version: press the two inner corners by the nose while holding the breath; when the breath must pass, stop, exhale and start again; kept up, 'the eyes can see through'. The syntax of 覺令見光 is terse; it could also be read 'until you notice light being seen'.
  - passage (mode; Juan 10, 服霧之法 (method of ingesting mist), base-edition pp. 001b-002a (Part A lines 574-586)): At dawn in the quiet bedchamber, sitting or lying as you like, first shut the eyes and look within, vaguely, so that it is as if you saw the five viscera. Then breathe out through the mouth twenty-four times, doing this with eyes half closed, making the eyes see vapours of five colours winding about each other thickly over the face; take these five-coloured vapours into the mouth fifty times, swallow saliva sixty times, whisper the incantation, knock the teeth seven times, swallow seven times, then open the eyes. The wording moves from 'as if seeing' (使如見) for the viscera to 'make the eyes see' (使目見) for the vapours.
  - passage (method; Juan 9, 雲林王夫人 on the Siming eye and ear method, base-edition pp. 011a-012a (Part A lines 237-254)): Lust darkens the eyes and much worry shuts the ears; these two ills come from within. To see through and observe spirits, first wash the heart and cut off thoughts. Lying at night, shut the eyes tight, face east, and with the heel of the thumbs and palms rub from the eyes to the ear-gates so that the palms meet at the nape, twenty-seven times; hold in mind that purple, blue and crimson vapours come out of the eyes before them: this is 'pressing the three plain clouds inwardly to bathe the pupils'. Say the 48-character incantation silently, swallow fifty times, then open the eyes. May be done seated, in the hours of living breath. After about a year eyes and ears are keen; after long practice one sees through a thousand li and sees spirits.
  - passage (testimony; Juan 9, 東卿司命 on the 太上明堂玄眞上經 sun and moon method, base-edition pp. 018a-018b (Part A lines 388-405)): Under a pure fast without grain, hold the sun (by day) and moon (by night) in the mouth, the size of a ring: sun red with purple light and nine rays, moon yellow with white light and ten rays; swallow the fluid of the rays, privately and without count. When not practising, let sun and moon go back to dwell in the Mingtang of the face, sun left and moon right, their breath joined with the pupils. Jiwei long ago fasted three years; only when his sincerity was exhausted in single thought did he get it, whereupon divine light shone on his body and he then received the scripture. He had no text, only this oral formula.
  - passage (caution; Juan 10, 上清眞人馮延壽口訣, first item, base-edition pp. 018a-018b (Part A lines 939-951)): The study of life must begin with curing illness; without that, dietetics and breath practice do the body no good. The adept Wang Zhongfu practised inhaling the two luminaries and feeding on dawn-glow for over forty years and noticed no benefit at all, while his son did the same and ascended in eighteen. The Perfected of the Southern Peak told him the reason: grave illness, the brain palace depleted, fluids not flowing. He took medicine, kept practising, and ascended eighteen years later. Tao Hongjing marks this section as not from autograph revelation manuscripts but consistent with them.
- new leads: 陶弘景《登眞隱訣》 (Dengzhen yinjue, DZ 421): Tao's own note says the detailed measures for the eye-pressure and other methods of this chapter are written out there. | 《上清明堂玄眞經訣》 / 太上明堂玄眞上經 (DZ 424): the fuller sun-and-moon-in-the-mouth method summarised here; Tao says the main text is in the Mao biography. | 《皇天上清金闕帝君靈書紫文上經》 (DZ 639): the 'Purple Texts' sun and moon ingestion that the Zhen'gao refers to as 寳書/紫文. | Thomas E. Smith, Declarations of the Perfected (Three Pines Press): English translation with notes; in copyright, not opened.


# STREAM: Jewish mystical practice (jewish)

## [jew-abulafia-or-ha-sekhel] Or ha-Sekhel (Light of the Intellect)
Abraham Abulafia; c. 1285; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep_marginal; relevance 2, practicality 3, confidence high (High for the verdict that the instructions (part 8) and the theory of apprehension (parts 9-10) contain no seen-light claim: those were read in full in Hebrew. The remainder was covered by keyword windows, so a stray remark outside the light and vision vocabulary could have been missed. One clause in 8:60 is corrupt in the transcription. The two new leads are from my general knowledge of the school, not from this file.)
- mode: ambiguous. No light is reported as seen anywhere in the passages read. 'Light' [אור השכל] is consistently the Maimonidean figure for the Active Intellect: sunlight entering a dark house (Introduction), the bat blinded by the sun (10:9), the east 'whence light goes out to the world' as a gloss on the Intellect (4:4, 8:60). What the book does say about seeing is theoretical: the influx [שפע] is 'seen' in the intellect, which is intellectual apprehension; it then leaps to the imagination [דמיון], which sees a corporeal-seeming spiritual likeness called 'man' or 'angel'; and 'at certain times' it passes on to the sensed [מורגש] (10:20-21). That is an imaginal-crossing doctrine for figures, not a report of light.
- eyes: unspecified; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET unclear: The book gives a complete induction procedure, but for cleaving [דבקות] to the Active Intellect and receiving the influx, not for light. It promises that fear and awe give way to the soul's great joy in its apprehension; it never says what, if anything, will be seen and offers nothing to a person who sees nothing. Prerequisites are stated: strength in wisdom [חכמה] and in received tradition [קבלה], and a mind not gripped by worldly vanities; without these one must not pronounce the Name. No time scale. The influx is described as compelling once it comes, unless inner or outer obstacles block the receiver (Introduction; 10:20).
- techniques: sound_or_mantra, breath_control, movement, letter_or_symbol_contemplation, sensory_restriction; phenomena: figures_or_forms
- conditions: posture: Seated, wrapped in clean or new white garments over all one's clothes, or in the tallit, head crowned with tefillin.; gaze_or_eye_position: Face turned due east, neither down nor up, at the start; no instruction about the eyes. The head then moves with each vowel.; setting: Alone in a set-apart place where one's voice cannot be heard by anyone.; duration_and_schedule: Not given as clock time. Measured in breaths: one long breath per letter-pair; at most 2 silent breaths within a pair in the reversed tables; at most 5 at the end of each row; at most 25 between the 'page' of one letter of the Name and the next.; prerequisites: Adorn oneself; purify heart and soul of every thought of this world; competence in wisdom and Kabbalah; freedom from preoccupation with worldly vanities.; other: Think that in that hour the soul separates from the body, that one dies to this world and lives to the world to come.
- stages: אימה, יראה ופחד (dread, awe and fear): The mood in which every letter is to be chanted. -> שמחת הנפש בהשגתה (the soul's joy in its apprehension): Joins the fear as the practice takes hold; the only result named in the instructions. Nothing visual. -> שפע: intellect, then imagination, then sense: Theory in part 10, not a practice stage: the influx is first an intellectual apprehension, overflows into the imagination as a seen figure ('man' or 'angel'), and occasionally reaches the senses.
- with the light: Not addressed: no light is described. For imaginative visions generally, the book ranks intellectual apprehension above anything imagined or sensed and treats the figure seen as the imagination's rendering of the influx.
- obstacles/fixes: A slip or a repetition inside a row: go back to the head of that row and say it again until it is said correctly. | Between the tables of the four letters do not interrupt with speech or with thought, even while taking the permitted breaths. | Inner or outer obstacles in the receiver block the influx (stated in general terms, no remedy beyond the preparation).
- cautions: If you sense that your intellect is weak in wisdom or in Kabbalah, or that your thoughts are strongly held by the vanities of the age, do not pronounce the Name, lest you add to your sin. | The imagination never apprehends any true existent; its third kind of product, pure invention, misleads and corrupts every true apprehension (10:17). | The human intellect has a limit; one who tries to pass it becomes confused and his apprehension turns imaginary (9:4).
- safety flags: breath_retention
- interpretation: Light is the Active Intellect [שכל הפועל], also called Shekhinah or holy spirit, whose radiance is too great for the embodied soul, as sunlight is for a bat. What a prophet sees is the influx clothed by his own imagination in a bodily likeness.
- practice: This source teaches the Abulafian recitation, not a light practice. Adorn yourself and withdraw to a place where no one can hear you. Empty heart and soul of every worldly thought and consider that your soul is now leaving the body: you die to this world and live to the next. Sit facing due east, wrapped in clean white garments or tallit, wearing tefillin. Take each letter of the Name paired with aleph, in the vowel written in the tables, and chant the pair on one unbroken breath drawn out as long as you can, in dread and awe, until joy joins the fear. The melody and the head follow the vowel: holam, raise the head slowly from centre-east to upright, then bow to the ground once; hiriq, draw downward, pulling the upper power into you; shuruq, straight forward; tsere, left to right; qamats, right to left. Keep the breath limits between pairs, rows and tables; after any slip restart the row. The text names no visual result.
  - passage (method; File section 'Ohr HaSekhel 8', paragraph 59 (source line 576); Abulafia's own heading at para. 54: part 5 (ה'), matter 3, sign 200): Preparation: adorn yourself, seclude yourself in a special place where your voice is not heard by another, purify heart and soul of all thoughts of this world, and think that in that hour your soul parts from your body, that you die to this world and live to the world to come, which is the source of life and is the Intellect.
  - passage (method; File section 'Ohr HaSekhel 8', paragraph 60 (source line 578)): Posture and procedure: seated, wrapped in clean or new white garments or the tallit, head crowned with tefillin, facing the east 'whence light goes out to the world' (the only mention of light in the instructions, and it is directional symbolism). One breath per letter-pair, as long as the breath allows, chanted in dread, awe and fear until the soul's joy in its great apprehension joins in. Melody and head movement take the shape of the vowel point: holam upward from the middle of the east, then one prostration; hiriq downward, drawing the upper power to cleave to you; shuruq a middle pull straight ahead; tsere left to right; qamats right to left. A clause following the qamats instruction ('שלא תוכל לסבלו ...') is corrupt in this transcription and I could not construe it; it may concern an effect too strong to bear and a second recitation 'in the manner of running and returning'.
  - passage (caution; File section 'Ohr HaSekhel 8', paragraph 60 (source line 578)): Prerequisite and warning: if you feel your intellect is weak in wisdom or in Kabbalah, or your thoughts are firmly in the vanities of the time, do not pronounce the Name, lest you sin further. Also here: up to 25 breaths between the table of one letter of the Name and the next, provided there is no interruption by speech or by thought.
  - passage (mode; File section 'Ohr HaSekhel 10', paragraph 20 (source line 644); Abulafia's heading at para. 18: 'the necessity of the influx that moves one to speak and compose'): Because man is compounded of many powers, the influx must be 'seen' in his intellect, and that seeing is called a thought, intellectual apprehension. The influx then leaps on to the imagination, which must apprehend in its own way and sees what it sees as an imagined, spiritual, shared likeness of corporeality; that power is called 'man' or 'angel'. Because the multitude trusts what is sensed more than what is imagined, Scripture had to speak as though all prophecies were sense perceptions.
  - passage (mode; File section 'Ohr HaSekhel 10', paragraph 21 (source line 646)): The intellectual apprehension is the highest; sense is matter to imagination and imagination matter to intellect. Yet the thing that flows down does at certain times pass over into the sensed. This one clause is the book's only admission that the experience can become perceptual; no detail of what is then perceived is given.
  - passage (interpretation; File section 'Ohr HaSekhel 10', paragraph 9 (source line 621); Abulafia's heading at para. 8: 'the form of the apprehension of the Intellect'): The form of the Intellect is subtler than subtle and purer than pure; because of the abundance of its light and bright radiance, which illuminates the whole world, its beauty is hidden, as the sun's light is hidden from the eyes of a bat or of one with sore eyes. The hindrance is not in the light but in the weakness of the receiver: the soul, being material, weakens when it tries to contemplate the giver of the light of the intellect. Explicitly a simile ['על דרך משל'] a few lines on. (The saved file reads נעטלף, presumably for עטלף.)
- new leads: Abulafia, Chayei ha-Olam ha-Ba and Sefer ha-Cheshek: the same recitation with stated results (trembling, anointing sensation, vision of a figure); to be checked for seen light. | Sha'arei Tzedek (anonymous disciple of Abulafia): first-person account of light appearing in the room at night after letter combination; the testimony this manual lacks.

## [jew-vital-shaarei-kedushah] Sha'arei Kedushah (Gates of Holiness), Part 3 gates 1-8 and the stub of Part 4
Hayyim Vital; c. 1572-1578 (composition, per Sefaria); English 2013; classical_text; rights in_copyright; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 3, practicality 4, confidence medium (Read in digest plus one grep; Gate 8 was seen in source, not digest, and its details are summarised from that grep.)
- mode: imaginal_crossing. Vital says outright that the practitioner first only pictures the ascent in the imagination ('he does not actually see them, he sees them in his mind'). The light then descends into the imaginal faculty, where it is depicted 'as though he literally saw' it, and may be copied outward to the five physical senses so that he sees and hears physically. The crossing from imagined to perceived is the point of the procedure, but he gives no description of the visual quality.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: Induction: a graded preparation (repentance, commandments, set times, immersion, then solitude in a secluded house after midnight, eyes closed, thought emptied as if dead, yearning to ascend, picturing the soul rising through the worlds). To someone who feels nothing: he is not yet fit, so he strengthens his service and sanctity and returns after a period. Response is conditional on fitness: the upper levels take no notice of a person unfit to draw light down. Early prophetic practice depended on kavvanot and yichudim transmitted by a teacher. No time scale is given beyond 'after a period of time'.
- techniques: visualization_crossing, sensory_restriction, devotion_or_transmission, sound_or_mantra, fasting_or_austerity; phenomena: figures_or_forms, light_in_the_room
- conditions: posture: not specified in digest; gaze_or_eye_position: eyes closed; setting: secluded house, undisturbed by voices or birdsong, after midnight, in purity; duration_and_schedule: set times prepared for; repeat after a period if nothing is felt; prerequisites: five stages of refinement: repentance, full observance, fixed times for sanctification, ritual immersion and clean garments, ingrained good character; other: transmitted kavvanot and yichudim in the prophetic schools
- stages: divestment (hitpashtut): Imagination stopped from picturing worldly things; thought emptied as if the soul had left. -> ascent in imagination: Imagination redirected to depict ascent through the roots of the soul to its upper source; forms of the lights are engraved in thought, not seen. -> descent of light: Light is drawn down through the levels to the intellectual soul and then the imagination, which depicts it in physical form as if seen with the eyes; sometimes as an angel. -> outer senses: The imaged vision may be copied to the physical senses, so that he sees, hears, smells and speaks.
- with the light: Test it: judge whether the spirit is pure or an admixture of good and evil, by whether its words are true and in accord with Torah; if not, push it off and strengthen devotion. Do not adhere to the husk-lights passed through.
- obstacles/fixes: Feeling nothing: not yet fit, so increase service and sanctity and return later. | Imagination persisting in worldly images: it must be stopped before it is redirected. | Mixed or false visitation: uproot it and refine until all words are true.
- cautions: Practical Kabbalah (use of names to command angels) is forbidden; seeing is mixed truth and falsehood in the lowest world. | Ben Azai and Ben Zoma were harmed in the Pardes. | Prophecy proper has ceased; only the Holy Spirit from Yetzirah downward remains.
- safety flags: transmission_required_by_tradition, tradition_warns_against_seeking
- interpretation: The light is an influx from the upper Sefirot and the root of the soul, drawn down through the worlds and rendered by the imagination as seen form.
- practice: Prepare by repentance, observance, set times of sanctification, immersion and clean clothes, until good traits are ingrained. Then retire to a quiet secluded house after midnight, close the eyes and stop the mind picturing worldly things, as if dead to the world. With strong yearning, use the imagination to picture the soul leaving the body and ascending through the upper worlds to its root. Intend to draw light and influence from the Sefirot down through the same levels to yourself, and picture yourself as present there. Where the light arrives it may be depicted vividly, as though seen with the physical eyes, and may pass to the outer senses. If nothing is felt, you are not yet fit; deepen devotion and repeat later. When something comes, test it for truth and uproot it if mixed.
  - passage (method; Part 3, Gate 5, para 8, source lines 289-298): Divestment means the imagination is stopped from picturing worldly things, as if the soul had left, and is then turned to depict the ascent.
  - passage (mode; Part 3, Gate 5, para 8, source lines 289-298): In the ascent phase the lights are only imagined, not seen; he sees them in the mind as with ordinary imagination.
  - passage (stages; Part 3, Gate 5, para 10, source lines 289-298): The descending light reaches the imagination, which depicts it in physical form, as if literally seen with the eyes.
  - passage (onset; Part 3, Gate 5, para 18, source lines 316-325): The upper powers respond only to a person fit to draw light down; otherwise his thought is ignored.
  - passage (caution; Part 3, Gate 6, para 8, source lines 332-345): The ascent did not occur by itself but through intentions and unifications transmitted by a teacher.
- new leads: Part 3 Gate 8 paras 8-11 not in digest; read in source by grep only

## [jew-zohar-closed-eye-colours] Zohar: the hidden shining colours seen by closing and rolling the eye (II:23a-b, I:97a-b Sitrei Torah, I:42a-43a Heikhalot, I:18b, II:69a)
Zohar (traditionally R. Shimon bar Yochai; redacted in Castile, late 13th century, circle of Moses de Leon); c. 1280-1300 (composition); English 1931-34; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 2, confidence high (All five loci now read in the source, including the Aramaic-only Bereshit 66 passage the earlier digest missed; it adds a simile, not new method. Practice-vs-figure: real act used as demonstration, not a regimen.)
- mode: perceptual.
