The text locates the vision in the physical eye: 'close thine eye and press thine eyeball', 'radiating and luminous colours which can only be seen with closed eyes', seen in the rolling or turning of the eye. The open eye sees the non-luminous mirror; the closed eye sees the luminous one. The Soncino editor reads the colours as not attached to any material background.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: unclear
- ONSET induction: A one-line instruction with no prerequisites: close the eye and turn/roll the eyeball (Aramaic satim einakh ve-ascher galgalakh; Soncino 'press'), and shining colours appear that are given only to closed eyes. Sitrei Torah grades three ways of seeing, the third a shining mirror seen only in the rolling of the fully shut eye, which cannot be held. Bereshit 66 para 17 states the dependence physiologically: were it not for the eye's visible colours, the shining colours would not appear when the eye is shut and rolled. No time scale, no training. Practice or figure: the text treats it as a real, repeatable act that the reader is told to perform, but always as a demonstration ('ve-raza da', 'this mystery') of the relation of revealed to hidden Sefirot, never as a cultivated contemplative regimen; Bereshit 66 uses it explicitly as a simile ('like that sight seen in closing when the eye rolls'). Full seeing of the higher colours remains Moses's alone.
- techniques: eye_pressure_or_sense_closure, eye_position; phenomena: colour_fields, white, red, green, lamp_or_flame, rainbow
- conditions: gaze_or_eye_position: eye shut, eyeball pressed or rolled; prerequisites: none stated; other: Balaam looked at the light with a 'closed eye' and saw a small light as through a wall
- stages: three colours at a distance, narrowed, and in the rolling of the shut eye: Sitrei Torah: the third is a mirror of light seen only in the closed rolling eye; one cannot stay in that colour. -> non-luminous vs luminous mirror: The open eye sees the lightless mirror (seeing); the closed eye sees the luminous mirror (knowing). -> sparkling radiance in the rolled closed eye (zahara natzitz): Bereshit 66 para 16: the contents of the palaces are like the sight seen in closing when the eye rolls, where a sparkling radiance appears; what is not seen in that rolling is the spirit ruling over all.
- with the light: Use the visible colours as a means to apprehend the hidden ones; the Patriarchs knew the concealed colours through the revealed ones. The vision cannot be held, per Sitrei Torah.
- obstacles/fixes: The luminous colour cannot be kept in view (Sitrei Torah).
- cautions: Balaam saw only a thin streak of light through the husk, knowing not what he said; the vision is not trustworthy when the seer is impure. | Only Moses beheld the highest colours.
- safety flags: eye_pressure
- interpretation: The closed-and-rolled-eye colours are a physical demonstration of the luminous mirror above the visible, non-luminous colours; the Zohar uses this real effect as the model for the hidden Sefirot, for the radiance hidden in the pupil (I:18b), and for the linked palaces of prayer (I:42a-43a).
- practice: The Zohar's instruction is brief: close the eye and press or turn the eyeball, and radiant, shining colours appear that cannot be seen with open eyes. A parallel text distinguishes three ways of seeing: from afar with the eye narrowed, with the eye nearly shut and opened a little, and a radiance seen only in the rolling of the fully closed eye, which the seer cannot hold steady. The open eye shows only the lightless mirror. The visible colours, white, red and green in the bow, serve as a way to infer the hidden luminous ones. No preparation, duration or troubleshooting is given. Balaam's looking with a 'closed eye' shows the same method with a flawed seer.
  - passage (method; Zohar II:23a-b (Vaera 2), para 7, source lines 1-54): Close the eye and press the eyeball, and shining, luminous colours are discerned that can be seen only with closed eyes.
  - passage (mode; Zohar II:23a-b (Vaera 2), para 9, source lines 1-54): The closed eye sees the luminous mirror; the open eye sees the lightless one.
  - passage (stages; Zohar I:97a-b (Vayera 4), para 3, source lines 1-54): The third colour is the shining mirror seen only in the rolling of the eye when it is closed, and one cannot stay in it.
  - passage (caution; Zohar II:69a (Yitro 1), para 49, source lines 259-279): Balaam looked at the light with a closed eye, as through a partition, and saw only a thin streak of light without understanding it.
  - passage (mode; Zohar I:42a-43a (Bereshit 66), para 16, source line 339): Everything within the palaces is like the sight seen with the eye shut as it rolls, where a sparkling radiance appears; what is not seen in that rolling is the ruling spirit.
  - passage (interpretation; Zohar I:18b (Bereshit 7), para 6, source lines 132-133): Ezekiel's 'brightness round about' is the radiance hidden in the rolling of the eye's sight, which becomes the likeness of the Glory.


# STREAM: Sufi practice (sufi)

## [suf-dara-shikoh-compass-of-truth] The Compass of Truth, or Risala-i-Haq-Numa
Muhammad Dara Shikoh (1615-1659); 1646 (1055-56 AH); English tr. 1912; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep_marginal; relevance 3, practicality 4, confidence medium (Read in digest only; the one illumination passage is OCR-garbled and appears to describe bodily sensation rather than seen light.)
- mode: imaginal_crossing. The first practice is explicitly mind's-eye work: eyes closed, attention on the heart, 'with the eyes of the heart see the picture of the Master'. The translator glosses these as 'thought-forms'. The form seen after closing the eyes is then treated as the soul of the form and a key to Malakut, which hints at crossing into perceived forms, but the text never says the image becomes seen in the visual field. The only light report ('subtle illumination in thy heart, and in all thy body') is a bodily sensation, not clearly a seen light.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET induction: For a seeker with nothing: find a solitary corner, form a picture of a revered faqir or loved one, close the eyes, fix attention on the heart. Long persistence is said to make the picture 'definite and clear' and then to show pictures never seen before. Alongside this comes breath retention with La ilaha / Illa 'llah. No time scale except the teacher's years of breath-holding. Grace is mentioned in general terms; transmission through a chain is stressed for the breath practice.
- techniques: visualization_crossing, breath_control, sound_or_mantra, eye_pressure_or_sense_closure, posture_or_body_lock, devotion_or_transmission; phenomena: figures_or_forms, body_or_objects_luminous
- conditions: posture: Sit with elbows on knees; thumbs close the ears, index fingers hold the eyelids shut without pressing the eyeballs, ring and little fingers seal the lips, middle fingers work the nostrils.; gaze_or_eye_position: Eyes closed; attention on the heart (cedar heart, left chest), or on the colourless spherical heart in the brain if distractions trouble.; setting: Retired, solitary spot; cave or desert for the sound practice.; duration_and_schedule: Hold the breath as long as comfortable and lengthen slowly; the teacher held one breath from evening to morning prayer for fifteen years.; prerequisites: A master or lineage; the practice is said to come by authentic descent.; other: Recite La ilah on inhaling and Il allah on exhaling.
- stages: Alam-i-misal: Thought-picture of the master seen with the heart's eyes; when clear, it opens onto Malakut. -> Alam-i-malakut: Forms of prophets, saints, angels appear in the polished heart-mirror; do not rest or seek miracles there. -> breath-retention effects: Heat, buoyancy, delight and a subtle illumination in heart and whole body.
- with the light: Observe the Malakut appearances minutely, but do not stay entangled or desire clairvoyance and miracles; the aim lies beyond.
- obstacles/fixes: Distracting 'dangers' in the cedar heart: move attention to the colourless spherical heart in the brain. | Higher fix: treat the distracting form as one's own self so it ceases to be a danger. | Breath expelled too fast harms the lungs; expel slowly.
- cautions: Do not be absorbed in the Malakut world or seek phenomena and miracles. | Breath retention is not to be pushed to suffocation.
- safety flags: breath_retention, sleep_deprivation
- interpretation: The images are thought-forms of the plane of counterparts, a gateway to the subtle world whose forms are souls; the heart is a mirror that brightens as rust is removed.
- practice: Sit alone in a quiet corner and picture a revered teacher or a loved person. Close the eyes, fix attention on the heart and see the form with the eyes of the heart. Keep at it long and steadily until the picture is definite and clear, and expect further, unfamiliar forms to follow. For a stronger practice, with a teacher's authorisation, sit with elbows on the knees and seal ears, eyelids (without pressing the eyeballs), lips and nostrils with the fingers. Inhale through the left nostril reciting La ilah, draw the breath from the brain down to the heart, close both nostrils and hold as long as comfortable, then release slowly through the right nostril reciting Il allah, and alternate sides. After some time heat, buoyancy, delight and a subtle illumination in heart and body are said to arise, with inner sounds. If distraction troubles, shift attention to the brain centre.
  - passage (method; Ch. I, source lines 374-413): Close the eyes, fix attention on the heart, and see the master's or beloved's picture with the heart's eyes.
  - passage (mode; Ch. II, source lines 515-589): The form seen after closing the eyes is treated as the soul of the form, not a body, and the thought-picture plane is the key to Malakut.
  - passage (method; Ch. II, source lines 779-867): Eyes are held shut by the index fingers resting on the lids, explicitly without pressing the eyeballs, during breath retention.
  - passage (development; Ch. II, source lines 935-971): After some practice of breath retention, heat, buoyancy, delight and a subtle illumination in heart and body are felt.
- new leads: Mullah Shah Badakhshi's breath-retention lineage (Qadiri)

## [suf-fleischer-farbige-lichterscheinungen-1862] Ueber die farbigen Lichterscheinungen der Sufi's (translation of a passage from an anonymous Arabic guide to the Sufi life, Leipzig Stadtbibliothek MS 187)
Anonymous Sufi author (after 'Ala' al-Dawla Simnani, d. 1336), translated and introduced by Heinrich Leberecht Fleischer; original after 1336; German 1862, reprinted 1888; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 2, confidence medium (German translation of an anonymous Arabic manual, OCR with doubled spaces; Fleischer's own preface is dismissive.)
- mode: ambiguous. The author says coloured lights appear to travellers and that 'one or another Sufi undoubtedly really saw these colours'; the shining black seems to 'descend from the head'. He contrasts them with what one sees with the bodily eye and the eye of the spirit, and calls them a net that clothes inner ideas in the colours of sense perception. This points to seen light, but the passage never states eyes open or closed or placement in the visual field.
- eyes: unspecified; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET development_only: No induction is given. The lights simply appear to some travellers, change as they move between stations, and are best ignored. Some people see none and still behold God through strong faith. Older masters taught no graded sequence of colours.
- techniques: attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena; phenomena: colour_fields, black_light, blue, red, white, gold_yellow, green, smoke_mist_cloud
- conditions: prerequisites: Travelling the Sufi path through graded stages of purification; guidance of a tested master.
- stages: body purification: smoke-coloured grey light (Simnani's scheme) -> soul purification: pure blue -> heart purification: pure carnelian red -> mind/temperament purification: pure white -> spirit purification: pure yellow -> hiddenness: shining black that seems to descend from the head; underlying colour is a white purer than before -> divinity: pure green
- with the light: Refuse and ignore. Turn away from the lights; do not sort them, wait for them, or rest in them. Colours are not proof of progress.
- obstacles/fixes: Absorption in or expectation of particular colours draws the mind from God; fix by turning away. | Seeing a colour alongside a mental vision of something higher can confuse and hold back a capable traveller.
- cautions: Best to be dismissive of the coloured lights; they belong to the human subject, appear and vanish. | Whoever stops at any of them and is content is shut out from the eternal colourless divine light. | Self-appointed guides without a tested master have fallen into conceit through a single light. | The uncreated light is colourless and has no moon or sun form.
- safety flags: tradition_warns_against_seeking
- interpretation: The lights arise from the hidden interior of the human spirit itself, come and go in time, and are clothed in sensory colours as a net to catch inner ideas; the true divine light has no colour.
- practice: This is a warning text rather than a method. Travellers on the Sufi path may see coloured lights that change as they pass through stages: in one later scheme smoke-grey, blue, carnelian red, white, yellow, a shining black sinking from the head, and green. The author advises not to attend to them, not to separate or classify them, and not to await a given colour at a given time, since this pulls the heart from God. Seeing colours singly or mixed does not show that a stage has been passed. The real sign is that the light so pervades every side of the person that he is as if annihilated or beside himself. Some see no lights and arrive by strong faith. Follow a tested master and do not take pride in any light.
  - passage (caution; Fleischer p. 441, source lines 76-90): The best course is to turn away from the coloured lights, which belong only to the human subject and now appear, now not.
  - passage (onset; Fleischer p. 447, source lines 290-310): One moved by pure strong faith sees none of these lights and beholds God without anything sensibly perceived.
  - passage (stages; Fleischer p. 447, source lines 312-325): Undoubtedly one or another Sufi actually saw these colours, including the shining black that seems to sink from the head.
  - passage (development; Fleischer p. 448, source lines 361-370): Seeing colours is no sign of passing a stage; the sign is that the light pervades every part of the person so fully that he is as if annihilated.
  - passage (interpretation; Fleischer pp. 448-449, source lines 372-394): These lights spring from the hidden interior of the human spirit, arise and pass in time, and are clad in the colours of sense.

## [suf-ghazali-ihya-dhikr-seclusion-method] Ihya' 'ulum al-din, Book 21 (Wonders of the Heart): the Sufi method of seclusion and dhikr, as translated in Macdonald, Lecture IX
Abu Hamid al-Ghazali (d. 1111); c. 1100; English 1909; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 4, confidence medium (Translated through Macdonald (1909); the main dhikr passage was read by a targeted sed, not in the digest windows.)
- mode: ambiguous. The light 'shines out in his heart' like a lightning flash, with no statement whether it is seen with the eye; Ghazali elsewhere has the Sufi 'while awake' see angels and prophets' souls and hear them. His account of mirror-gazers says forms appear on a cloudlike curtain between gazer and surface, which he calls soul apprehension 'not really apprehension by vision' while formed 'as though according to sense'. So he marks the vision as sense-like but not ocular.
- eyes: unspecified; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: Induction: sever worldly ties, sit alone in a corner with only obligatory duties, repeat 'Allah, Allah' until the tongue stops, then until the word's letters and shape leave the heart and only the meaning remains. Up to there it depends on the will; then one can only wait for God to open. Light cannot be forced: 'to bring the mercy of God does not stand in his will or choice'. Elsewhere, light is 'cast into the breast' suddenly and should be watched for. No time scale beyond persistence.
- techniques: sound_or_mantra, concentration_nonvisual, sensory_restriction, devotion_or_transmission, scrying_surface; phenomena: lightning_flashes, all_pervading_light, figures_or_forms, smoke_mist_cloud
- conditions: posture: Sit alone in a corner.; setting: Solitude; seclusion, minimal duties, no Qur'an recitation or study during the practice.; duration_and_schedule: Continuous repetition until the tongue's motion ceases and the word's form drops away.; prerequisites: Cutting ties to family, wealth, knowledge, rank; a heart indifferent to existence or non-existence of things.; other: Nothing but God must enter the mind.
- stages: tongue stops: Word seems to flow from the tongue, then motion ceases. -> word's form removed: Letters and shape leave the heart, only the meaning cleaves to it. -> light of the real: Unstable at first like lightning, returns, sometimes abides long or short, sometimes similar appearances follow one another.
- with the light: Wait and watch for it; do not reach for it. Keep to the dhikr and ward off whisperings. Divine openings are to be met when they come.
- obstacles/fixes: Satan's whisperings: continued will and persistence ward them off. | Hallucination from hunger, solitude and expectation (reported by a sceptical Isfahan philosopher in Macdonald's chapter on jinn).
- cautions: Light comes from God's mercy, not from will or proof. | Seen forms of angels or devils are usually symbols, constructions of the imagination, not their real forms. | Scryers who gaze at mirrors or water are the weaker kind of the diviner type.
- safety flags: sleep_deprivation, fasting
- interpretation: The light is God's casting into the breast, the key to knowledge, a gift of the divine bounty reaching those who are prepared; forms seen are images (mithal) that link sense to the unseen.
- practice: Cut all anxieties about family, wealth, rank and knowledge until existence and non-existence of things are equal to you. Sit alone in a corner, doing only the obligatory duties, no Qur'an reading or study, and let nothing but God enter the mind. Say 'Allah, Allah' continuously, keeping the thought on it, until the tongue's motion stops and the word seems to flow of itself. Persist until the letters and shape of the word leave the heart and only the meaning remains. Everything up to here is yours to do. Then wait, with no further effort, for what God opens. The light of the real may flash like lightning, return, sometimes stay, sometimes briefly. Keep going; do not dwell on any one appearance.
  - passage (onset; Lecture IX, source lines 9689-9725): If the seeker follows the course of dhikr in solitude he may be sure the light of the real will shine in his heart, at first like lightning.
  - passage (method; Lecture IX, source lines 9689-9725): Say Allah, Allah without ceasing until the tongue stops and the word's form leaves the heart.
  - passage (mode; Intercourse with the Unseen, source lines 3960-4020): Scryers gaze until the mirror vanishes, and a cloudlike curtain appears on which forms show themselves; Ghazali says this is soul-apprehension, not ocular.
  - passage (interpretation; Lecture VII, source lines 6894-6931): Certainty returned by a light God cast into the breast, not by proof; the opening is light, and unveiling is to be sought from it and watched for.

## [suf-ibn-arabi-risalat-al-anwar] Risalat al-anwar fima yumnah sahib al-khalwa min al-asrar (Treatise of Lights: on the secrets granted to one who undertakes retreat)
Muhyi al-Din Ibn 'Arabi (d. 638/1240); 602/1205-6 (Konya); edition 2000; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 4, confidence medium (Arabic OCR with interleaved editor's footnotes; the stage sequence is reconstructed from the digest and the search agent's hints.)
- mode: ambiguous. Ibn 'Arabi himself separates sensory unveiling from imaginal: if you see the form of a person and close your eyes, the vision that persists is in your imagination; if it vanishes, the perception was attached to the place where you saw it. This makes the early unveilings explicitly open-eyed or perceptual-sensory, then he moves the practitioner to imaginal and then abstract-meaning stages. The later lights (sparks, ascendants, 'a light in which you see none but yourself') are not tied to eyes open or shut.
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: A full entry route: study and discipline before retreat, close the door on people, repeat the Name Allah (Allah, Allah), moderate diet to avoid imaginings from dryness, hold the creed that nothing is like Him. Unveilings are then 'raised' in a fixed order if the practitioner does not stop at each one. No time scales given. Nothing is said about the person who sees nothing, but the order and test imply the first stage is sensory sight through walls and darkness.
- techniques: sound_or_mantra, concentration_nonvisual, sensory_restriction, fasting_or_austerity, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena, devotion_or_transmission; phenomena: lightning_flashes, sparks_or_points, stars, figures_or_forms, all_pervading_light, light_in_the_room
- conditions: gaze_or_eye_position: Eye test for distinguishing sensory from imaginal vision: close the eyes.; setting: Khalwa, a closed room, door shut to people and family.; duration_and_schedule: Continuous dhikr; no durations.; prerequisites: Training of character and religious knowledge before retreat; a shaykh if possible; control of imagination.; other: Rich diet without animal products, avoid satiety and excessive hunger; keep temperament balanced.
- stages: sensory unveiling: Seeing what people do in their houses, through walls and darkness; do not disclose it. -> imaginal unveiling: Forms and offered drinks (water, milk, honey; avoid wine); test by closing eyes. -> flashes: Flashes (lawa'ih) and frightening visions. -> light scattering sparks: A light that throws off sparks, from which one seeks cover; keep to dhikr and do not fear. -> lights of the ascendants: A further light of ascendants (tawali') and the wider order of worlds. -> light of the self: A light in which you see none but yourself, bringing great ecstasy.
- with the light: Do not stop with any of it; take each unveiling with courtesy, keep the dhikr, and push on. Lingering with an appearance loses the rest.
- obstacles/fixes: Fear at the spark-scattering light: do not fear, remain in dhikr, and no harm will reach you. | Forms that claim 'I am God': say 'Glory to God' and continue the dhikr. | Hallucination from dryness: moderate diet and temperament. | Distinguish angelic arrivals from satanic ones by the after-effect: the first leaves coolness and calm, the second distress and confusion.
- cautions: Do not seek anything but God in the retreat; if a thing is offered, take it politely and do not stay with it. | Do not disclose hidden things you see; if you accuse people from what you see, suspect Satan. | Opening the door to visitors makes one a seeker of rank and cut off from God.
- safety flags: tradition_warns_against_seeking
- interpretation: The lights and visions are stations of an ordered ascent that the retreatant passes through; God is like nothing, so any form is only a veil.
- practice: Prepare by learning the religious obligations and training character. Then enter retreat, close the door to people and household, and repeat the Name 'Allah, Allah' constantly, adding nothing. Eat moderately, avoiding satiety and extreme hunger, because dryness breeds fancies. Hold two vows: nothing is like Him, so dismiss any form that claims divinity with 'glory to God'; and ask nothing but Him. If unveilings come, take them courteously and do not stop. When you see a person or an act, close the eyes: if the sight stays it is imagination, if it vanishes it was located perception. Later flashes and a spark-throwing light may frighten you; do not fear, keep to the dhikr.
  - passage (mode; Treatise, source lines about 540-560 (eye test)): Closing the eyes tests whether a sensory-seeming unveiling is imaginal (it persists) or located perception (it vanishes).
  - passage (development; Treatise, source lines about 636-638 (spark light)): If you do not stop, a light scattering sparks is raised; you seek cover; do not fear and persist in dhikr and no harm comes.
  - passage (caution; Treatise, source lines about 636-638): Do not fear and remain in dhikr when the spark-throwing light appears.
  - passage (stages; Treatise, source lines about 793-796 (light of the self)): A light is raised in which you see none but yourself, bringing great ecstasy and a pleasure in God never known before.

## [suf-ibn-ata-allah-miftah-al-falah] Miftah al-falah wa-misbah al-arwah (The Key to Salvation and the Lamp of Souls)
Ibn 'Ata' Allah al-Iskandari (d. 709/1309); c. 1300; OpenITI transcription dated 2022; classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 4, confidence high (Full reading of the practical first third; the remainder searched by vocabulary. OCR is noisy (letter confusions, dropped dots), so individual words in the quotes are garbled but the sense of every passage cited is clear, except the gaze clause at line 416-417, whose first half is damaged and reconstructed. The sources Ibn 'Ata' Allah is compiling from are not named by him at these points.)
- mode: ambiguous. The strongest statement uses the plain verb 'you see' [ترى أبدا أنواراً صاعدة وأخرى نازلة] and sits in a list of concrete bodily signs (head and limbs gripped as if chained, needle-pricks in the tongue, sounds of trumpets, water, wind and fire first heard 'in the circle of the head'), with eyes shut and, in retreat, a cell with no light-hole. That reads as perceived. But the text never says whether eye or heart sees; elsewhere it speaks of fruits that 'show themselves to the heart' [يلوح للقلب], of the 'eye of your heart' [بعين قلبك], and of forms that 'take shape' [يتمثل] from the imaginal. No colours, shapes, locations or sizes of light are described.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: Induction is by regime, not by a visual trick: a shaykh, graded formulas, a head-between-knees sitting with eyes closed in which 'lights come', and a dark cell with silent dhikr of the heart. The chapter on gradation opens: whoever keeps to the invocations, lights follow one another upon him and the veils over hidden things are drawn back. To the one who sees nothing: do not change formula until its fruit appears; fruits differ by person and by allotted provision [أرزاق], some get them awake, some in dreams; beware of haste. Prerequisites: repentance, training of character [رياضة] before retreat ('one whose opening comes before his training seldom amounts to a man'), lawful food (a clean interior makes the illumination stronger), correct creed. No time scale is given except that an incoming received in the silence after dhikr may build in a moment what thirty years of striving does not. The Name al-Nur is said to hasten opening for those in retreat, gradually and rarely completely. Light is a by-product and sign; attending to dhikr or heart is itself called a veil.
- techniques: sound_or_mantra, posture_or_body_lock, sensory_restriction, darkness_exposure, devotion_or_transmission, fasting_or_austerity, concentration_nonvisual, breath_control; phenomena: lights_rising_and_descending, fires_around_the_body, light_pouring_from_the_face, figures_or_forms, inner_sounds_trumpet_water_wind_fire, dream_signs
- conditions: posture: Two sittings are given. Adab chapter: cross-legged [متربعاً], facing the qibla if alone, palms on the thighs. Gradation chapter: humble sitting with the head placed between the knees.; gaze_or_eye_position: Eyes closed or lowered while keeping the attention fixed straight ahead (OCR damaged: 'رعض عينيه مع بقاء توجه / تصب عينيه', i.e. غض عينيه ... نصب عينيه); a disciple under a shaykh pictures the shaykh between his eyes [يخيل شيخه بين عينيه]. In the second sitting, eyes shut to sensible things.; setting: Perfumed place, clean lawful clothing. For retreat: a cell of body height, prostration length and sitting width, with no hole admitting daylight, away from voices, solid door, in an inhabited house; nobody should know what you are doing.; duration_and_schedule: Beginner: two set sessions, after the dawn prayer and after the sunset prayer; eleven repetitions of the full blessing-prayer after each obligatory prayer. In retreat: continuous, sleeping only when overcome, never lying down by choice. No number of days is stated.; prerequisites: Repentance and restitution; a realised shaykh who chooses the formula; training of character and a period of ordinary seclusion [عزلة] until solitude is no longer felt as effort; lawful and light food, no animal food in retreat, gradual reduction of eating and of water; constant ablution; courage (not startled by a great cry or a falling wall).; other: La ilaha illa'llah is said with full force, raising the phrase from above the navel, intending by 'la ilaha' to expel everything other than God from the heart and by 'illa'llah' to drive it into the pine-cone-shaped heart of flesh so that it spreads through the limbs. Opinions differ on whether to run the phrase together without gap (said to open the heart fastest) or to lengthen the 'la'. In retreat the formula is Allah Allah Allah (or Hu Hu), the tongue kept still, the heart saying it and the ear listening until 'the speaker' arises from the innermost.
- stages: ذكر اللسان (dhikr of the tongue): Letters without presence. Persisted in with absorption it burns the 'surplus parts' of the body; sound is first heard in the circle of the head (trumpets and cymbals), then water, wind, blazing fire, mills, horses' hooves, leaves in wind. -> ذكر القلب (dhikr of the heart): The heart joins the tongue, first by effort then by nature; the light of the heart burns passions and devils, limbs and breast fill with lights; when the tongue stops, the heart moves in the chest like a child in the womb, with a sound like the hum of bees. -> ذكر السر (dhikr of the innermost), the hidden dhikr: Absence from dhikr and from self [فناء]. Signs: the dhikr does not leave you when you leave it, it binds head and limbs, its fires and lights do not go out, you see lights rising and descending and clear fires around you, needle-pricks in the tongue, light flowing from the face. -> تمثل (forms from the higher world): When absorption becomes habitual: angels and spirits of prophets and saints appear in beautiful forms and convey realities. Marked as belonging to the beginning; one rises above the imaginal to the unveiled Real in everything.
- with the light: Treat it as a sign and a fruit, not an object. Do not turn to look at the dhikr or the heart: that turning is 'a distracting veil'. Report whatever arises, waking or in dream, to the shaykh, who uses it to decide when to change formula. When the inner speaker arises in retreat, do not leave the state you were in. Expect to pass beyond forms.
- obstacles/fixes: Nothing appears: stay on the same formula; the fruit may come in dream instead of waking, and differs by person. Do not hurry on from the blessing-prayer on the Prophet before its fruit shows. | Heart full of sense-images: a thousand repetitions register nothing; the negation is there to empty the heart first. Start with istighfar if there is a heavy past, then the blessing-prayer on the Prophet, described as the polish of hearts and as light that removes the darkness of the veil. | Unlawful food: dhikr still cleans it out but the illuminating effect is weaker, as water used on a soiled thing cleans without brightening. | Struggling inside the retreat (hunger, thirst, sleeplessness, cold, inner chatter, loneliness): this breaks the gathered state and no incoming arrives; go back out to ordinary seclusion and do the struggling there first. | Weak or agitated temperament: give la ilaha illa'llah in a fixed small dose and increase gradually. | Overwhelming states: the shaykh switches the Name (for example al-Jabbar or al-Mutakabbir against excess expansion; al-Batin, al-Kabir or al-Muta'al when nearness or outward manifestation threatens derangement).
- cautions: Too much of the strong dhikr before readiness, with a hot temperament, burns the practitioner and cuts him off. | Excess dryness of constitution in retreat produces imaginings and raving, as distinct from a true incoming. | Satanic incomings exist: they leave pain, agitation and confusion, where angelic ones leave coolness, pleasure and knowledge. Test by the sacred law. | Thoughts in the guise of worship, good works and love of wonders [حب الكرامات] stay with a person until he is sincere. | Most people perish through the self's deceptions in retreat; one who secludes himself but lets people seek him out is after status. | An opening that comes before character training rarely yields a sound person. | Thinking 'I have passed away' during absorption is itself an impurity.
- safety flags: transmission_required_by_tradition, fasting, prolonged_darkness, sleep_deprivation
- interpretation: Dhikr is a fire: where it finds fuel it burns, where it finds darkness it becomes light, where it finds light it is light upon light. The lights and fires are the visible side of that burning and of the heart's polishing, and a sign that the invocation has sunk from tongue to heart to innermost; blessings on the Prophet matter because lights poured on the prophets' spirits are reflected onto those bound to them.
- practice: Under a shaykh, begin with istighfar if needed, then the blessing-prayer on the Prophet, in two daily sessions after dawn and sunset prayers. Sit perfumed and in ablution, either cross-legged facing the qibla with palms on thighs, or with head between the knees; close the eyes to sensible things and, if you have a shaykh, picture him between your eyes and draw on his resolve. Add la ilaha illa'llah, said forcefully, lifted from above the navel and driven into the physical heart, emptying the heart of everything else on each repetition. After stopping, stay silent and present for what arrives. Move on (to subhana'llah, then the single Name) only when the fruit of the current formula has shown, awake or in dream, and tell the shaykh everything. For retreat, first train character and get used to solitude; then enter a body-sized cell with no chink of daylight, eat lightly, sleep only when overcome, and repeat Allah Allah with the heart while the tongue is still and the ear listens.
  - passage (mode; Part I, second untitled fasl after the muqaddima (signs that dhikr has reached the sirr), file lines 105-111, PageV00P004): Signs that the invocation has fallen into the innermost [السر]: the invoker is absent from both invocation and Invoked; if you leave the dhikr it does not leave you; it grips your head and all your limbs so that you are as if bound with chains and fetters; and its fires [نيران] do not die down nor its lights [أنوار] depart: you see continually lights rising and others descending, with clear fires blazing and burning around you. When the invoker falls silent the dhikr is like needles pricking the tongue, or as if the whole face were a tongue invoking by a light pouring from it. The verb is plain 'you see' [ترى] and the item sits in a list of bodily sensations; the text does not say whether eye or heart sees.
  - passage (method; Bab tadrij al-salik bi'l-adhkar (Gradation of the wayfarer through the invocations), 'hay'at al-julus lil-dhikr', file lines 822-825, PageV00P028): The sitting for dhikr: sit before your Master as a lowly, humble, needy servant; put your head between your knees and shut your eyes to sensible things [يسد عن المحسوسات عينيه]. By this sitting the heart is gathered and cleared of turbidity, and lights, flashes [اللوائح, OCR 'اللرانع'] and secrets come to it. Then seek refuge, say the basmala, state the intention and number, and begin.
  - passage (onset; Same bab, first fasl (fruits and signs of each dhikr), file lines 793-803, PageV00P027): Every dhikr has a fruit and a sign that the masters treat as decisive. The fruits are of two kinds: one that 'shows itself to the heart' [يلوح للقلب] in waking, and one the wayfarer sees in sleep. Some advance on a waking sign, some on a dream, some on both, which is the most complete. The fruits differ from person to person, according to each one's allotted provision [أرزاق], though they go back to fixed roots; nobody is moved from one dhikr to the next until the fruit proper to it has appeared on him. This is the text's answer to the person to whom nothing has appeared: stay with the same formula.
  - passage (method; Bab fi dhikr al-khalwa (On retreat), the cell, file lines 917-924, PageV00P032): Enter after a full ablution, with clean clothes and the intention of drawing near to God. The cell is as high as your standing height, as long as your prostration and as wide as your sitting; no hole lets daylight [الضوء] through into it; it is far from voices, with a solid short door, in an inhabited house, and it is better that someone sleep near the door. Move little, pray only the obligatory and regular prayers, face the qibla, stay in ritual purity; when you must go out, cover your eyes and ears and guard against outside air, which scatters you for a long time.
  - passage (caution; Bab fi dhikr al-khalwa, file lines 943-950, PageV00P033): Keep the constitution balanced: if dryness becomes excessive it leads to imaginings and raving [خيالات وهذيان]; only when it is the incoming [الوارد] itself that unbalances you is that the thing sought. The test between an angelic and a satanic incoming: the angelic is followed by coolness and pleasure, causes no pain, does not change your form, and leaves knowledge behind; the satanic is followed by disturbance in the limbs, pain and bewilderment, and leaves confusion.
  - passage (stages; Part I, second untitled fasl, file lines 97-102, PageV00P004): When absorption (which seldom lasts) becomes a settled habit, the invoker is raised to the higher world; the imprint of the malakut is stamped on him. The first things to take form for him [يتمثل] from that world are the substances of angels and the spirits of prophets and saints in beautiful form, through which some realities are poured on him. That belongs to the beginning, until his rank rises above the imaginal likeness [المثال; OCR 'الثال'] and he is met by the plain Real in everything. The sequence is: dhikr of the tongue, then of the heart with effort, then of the heart by nature, then the Invoked takes over and the dhikr is effaced.
- new leads: The retreat chapter (cell dimensions, conditions, 'Allah Allah' with the heart, the speaker arising from the sirr) runs very close to Ibn 'Arabi's Risalat al-anwar (Journey to the Lord of Power), which goes on to list what is unveiled stage by stage; worth reading alongside. This is my recognition, not stated in the text. | The signs passage (sounds in the head, fires and lights rising and descending, needle-pricks) matches Najm al-Din Kubra's Fawa'ih al-jamal and al-Usul al-'ashara; the 'eight conditions' are given here as 'the way of al-Junayd'.

## [suf-kubra-fawaih-al-jamal] Fawa'ih al-jamal wa-fawatih al-jalal (The Fragrances of Beauty and the Openings of Majesty)
Najm al-Din Kubra (d. 618/1221); c. 1200-1220; editions 1957 (Meier) and 1993 (Zaydan); classical_text; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 4, confidence high (Read in full in Arabic from two machine OCRs of different editions; both are noisy and several lines in each are unreadable, so every passage used here was checked in both files where possible. Meier section numbers are given where the OCR shows them clearly; sec. 49 is inferred from its neighbours. Anchors and quotes keep the OCR's errors (Persian letter forms, broken words). The darkness of the cell, often attributed to this text, is not stated in it.)
- mode: perceptual. Kubra opens with a closed-eye test: 'close your eyelids and look at what you see' [أطبق جفنيك وانظر ماذا ترى], and says the thing is to be seen 'in front of you while your eyelids are closed' [قدامك مع أنك مطبق جفنيك]. He gives the lights positions in the field (in front, above the head, at the right cheek, at the forehead, between the eyebrows, by the nose), says the cheek feels the heat of the red sun, and gives a second closed-eye check for the carnelian-red points at the forehead. He states the progression outright: a point first appears in trance [غيبة], then it appears when one is not absent but has the eyelids closed, then the stars appear in daylight with the eyelids not closed. He also separates this from imagery: vision is first by 'forms and imagination' [بالصور والخيال], and the colours appear only after the image-making faculties [المصورة والمخيلة] have tired of hunting and gone quiet. The organ is called inner sight [بصيرة], not the eye, and much of the higher material happens in trance or 'between waking and sleep'; a few reports are plain dream-visions.
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: The text speaks directly to the person who sees nothing (Meier sec. 2-6). If you close your eyes and say 'I see nothing', that is a mistake: you do see, but the 'darkness of your own existence' [ظلام الوجود] is too close to your inner sight to be noticed. To find it and see it in front of you with closed eyes, 'reduce something of your existence, or put some of it at a distance'. The means is striving [مجاهدة], fixed in three ways: (1) reduce food gradually; (2) give up your own choice to a trustworthy shaykh; (3) the way of Junayd, eight standing conditions: constant ablution, constant fasting, constant silence, constant seclusion [خلوة], constant dhikr of la ilaha illa'llah, constant binding of the heart to the shaykh and taking the knowledge of visionary events [علم الواقعات] from him, constant rejection of stray thoughts, constant non-objection to God (the tail of this clause is broken in the OCR; it ends 'or seeking refuge from a fire', which the editor's note takes as not asking for paradise or refuge from hell). A later passage repeats the list as the polishing of the heart-mirror. What is seen first is darkness, then a black cloud, so 'seeing nothing' is counted as the first stage. Prerequisites: a shaykh; sound intention (his own first retreat failed for wrong motive and he was put out on the eleventh day); sitting, wakefulness and dhikr kept up. Time scales: forty days is the assumed retreat length but the cell is to be entered as one's grave with no end date; his head pains resolved in under a week; tongue-dhikr gives way to heart-dhikr 'after a long period'. On forcing: the power of dhikr shows 'in proportion to one's service of dhikr and perseverance in it', so effort counts; but it is hidden while 'existence' is strong and shows when existence weakens 'through sleep or trance', and success in his own case he ascribes to God's help for his sincerity.
- techniques: sound_or_mantra, sensory_restriction, fasting_or_austerity, devotion_or_transmission, concentration_nonvisual, sleep_threshold, sky_or_space_gazing, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena; phenomena: darkness, black_cloud, smoke_mist_cloud, white, red, blue, green, yellow, gold_yellow, colour_fields, lamp_or_flame, lightning_flashes, sparks_or_points, stars, moon_disc, sun_disc, sphere_pearl_bindu, geometric_patterns, figures_or_forms, all_pervading_light, body_or_objects_luminous, spring_of_light, inner_sounds
- conditions: posture: Sitting kept up continuously [الجلوس]; in the middle stage one neither sleeps nor lies down. No detailed seat or hand position is given in this text.; gaze_or_eye_position: No gaze-fixing instruction. Eyes closed is the baseline test at the start and again for the carnelian points ('he turns to look with eyelids closed'); later the lights are seen with eyes open, by day. Lights are located in front, above the crown, at the right cheek, forehead, between the eyebrows and eyes, and beside the nose; the eye-circles show when one turns to look right or left.; setting: The retreat cell [خلوة], left only for a need; dhikr at night and before dawn; sometimes leaving the cell at night to walk in open country. This text does not itself say the cell is dark.; duration_and_schedule: Continuous. Forty days is the conventional term, but his shaykh Ammar told him never to tell himself he will come out after forty: 'this is your grave until the resurrection'. Kubra's own first retreat ended on day 11.; prerequisites: A shaykh to whom choice is surrendered and who interprets what is seen; purified intention; ablution, fasting, silence; gradual reduction of food.; other: Dhikr formula la ilaha illa'llah, first with the tongue, then in the heart, then in the 'secret' [سر]. Reduced sleep: early on one does not choose to sleep but is overtaken. Kubra also reports nights spent looking at the sky until it 'entered' him and he saw its stars within, then nights looking at the earth.
- stages: ظلام الوجود (darkness of existence): At first intense darkness. As it clears a little it takes the shape of a black cloud in front of you; red if it is 'Satan's throne'; when rights are kept and appetites spent it clears and turns white like a rain-cloud. -> النفس (the soul): When the soul shows, its colour is the blue of the sky and it wells up like water from a spring. -> المشاهدة الأدنى (the lower witnessing): Earth-contents seen in the unseen: deserts crossed, seas, rain, fires waded through, towns, wells; then a wide open space with clear air above and, at the limit of sight, colours: green, red, yellow, blue. Being at the bottom of a deep well, with green light at its floor, marks the end of created existence. -> الاستغراق الأول / الثاني / الثالث (three absorptions of dhikr): First: dhikr is heard from every part of the body (trumpets, drums, then a bee-hum), falling first in the circle of the head with pain. Second: dhikr opens a round door at the crown and from above come darkness, then fire, then green; when it reaches the heart a spring pouring light is seen in front, and an opening is felt in the right side. Third: the fires do not die and the lights do not leave; lights are always seen rising and descending with clear fires all round. -> علامات النفوس (signs of the three souls): Commanding soul: a large pitch-black circle rising in front, then like a cloud, then a crescent edge showing through, then a full crescent. Blaming soul: a red sun rising from the right cheek, its heat felt; sometimes by the ear, forehead or above the head. Soul at peace: a great spring-like circle pouring lights, or, in trance, a circle (the simile is unreadable in both OCRs) that rises to the face until the face disappears in it, so that the face itself is then the soul at peace. -> دوائر الوجه (circles of the face): At the far end: two circles of light of the eyes, each with a point at the centre, appearing whichever way one turns; a circle of the light of the Real between eyebrows and eyes, with no centre point; the circle of the spirit beside the nose. In mid-journey the eye-circles serve as a gauge: showing means a good state, hidden means existence has the upper hand; they change colour and size. -> المشاهدة العليا (the higher witnessing): Sun, moon, stars, zodiac and lunar mansions of subtler inner heavens. A point seen in trance grows into stars; then seen with closed eyes while present; then in daylight with eyes open. -> شيخ الغيب / المقدم / ميزان الغيب (the person of light): When the circle of the face is clear it pours light from between the eyes and eyebrows; then a face of light is seen opposite one's own, a sun moving behind its thin veil; then a whole person of light. He may meet you early on as a black figure, then vanish. -> لون العقيق (carnelian colour of the Great Intellect): After green, a black tablet at the forehead with red carnelian points that grow and shrink; checked by looking with the eyelids closed. 'At the ends, not the beginnings.' -> زجاج في زجاج (glass within glass): At night outside the cell solid things vanish and only glass in glass is seen, the limit of clarity; the sun of the spirit is then seen behind it.
- with the light: Read it as a gauge of one's state, not as a goal: steady green is settledness [تمكين], mixed and shifting colours are instability [تلوين], a murky colour is the darkness of existence; clear and bright means 'the scale has risen', dark or hidden means it has dropped. Tell the shaykh what is seen and take its meaning from him. Check each thing by what is tasted with it (ease and expansion against heaviness and constriction). Carry on with dhikr and do not rest in any station: there is always something higher. At the end the signs themselves are a veil to be thrown off.
- obstacles/fixes: Seeing nothing with closed eyes: you are seeing the darkness of your own existence; lessen it by striving (less food, surrender to a shaykh, the eight conditions). | Dark, smoky, slow fire with heaviness, tight chest and dhikr that will not come: this is Satan's fire; call inwardly 'O Helper of those who seek help, help me' [يا غياث المستغيثين أغثني] and do not engage or answer back. | Roaring sounds and severe pain in the head when dhikr enters the head: Kubra's shaykh told him to leave the cell and the dhikr lest he go mad or die; Kubra persisted in sincerity and it cleared within a week. | Terror and trembling in the well: take refuge in dhikr alone. | Retreat spoiled by motive (wanting to preach, books waiting outside): re-enter with purified intention, treating the cell as a grave and the clothes as a shroud. | Former company and old habits pulling one out of the cell: early on one's strength comes from the shaykh. | Being upset by any noise or sight during dhikr: his shaykh Abu'l-Najib warned against this; it shows one has made dhikr itself the object. | A thought of unknown source: ask the shaykh until one can taste the difference oneself; the satanic thought brings haste, no rest in the heart and a turn toward being seen. | The tongue's dhikr becoming a disturbance after long practice: stop the tongue and keep presence in the heart.
- cautions: Not every fire is the fire of dhikr; test by its clarity, upward speed and the state that comes with it. | Satan's promptings can take the form of worship, good works and love of powers and wonders. | Do not look at your own station of sincerity; that itself lets Satan in. | The soul does not die: like a snake's skin put in the sun it moves again. | The carnelian colour seen in trance 'shreds and shakes' the wayfarer; the weak cannot bear it. | Do not think the heaven you see is the last; and do not take the signs for the goal. | One pulled up by attraction alone [مجذوب], who has not walked the road, is not fit to guide. | Risk of madness or death in the stage of head-sounds is stated by his shaykh.
- safety flags: fasting, sleep_deprivation, transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: The covering is not outside the person: it is the darkness of his own existence, made of the four elements; 'our way is the way of alchemy', drawing the luminous subtle substance [اللطيفة النورانية] out from under them. Like sees only like, so each light or heaven seen means that part of oneself has been purified; soul, Satan, angel, heaven, earth and Throne are 'not things outside you'. Lights rising are of the heart, lights descending are of the Throne.
- practice: Close your eyes and look. If there is nothing, that nothing is the darkness of your own existence, too close to be noticed; the work is to thin it. Under a shaykh, enter a cell as if it were your grave and keep eight things without break: ablution, fasting, silence, seclusion, the dhikr la ilaha illa'llah, the heart tied to the shaykh, stray thoughts refused, no quarrel with God. Cut food gradually; sit, stay awake, repeat. Expect darkness, then a black cloud, then fire, then green; sounds and pain in the head as dhikr settles there; later a spring of light in front, coloured fields at the edge of sight, circles at the eyes and brow, suns and stars. Tell the shaykh what you see. Judge each appearance by what you feel with it: clear, fast, rising fire with ease is dhikr; smoky, slow fire with a tight chest is not. Steady green is a settled heart. First seen in trance, then with closed eyes, the lights end up visible by day with eyes open.
  - passage (onset; Meier sec. 2 (Meier file lines 277 and 307-311; Zaydan file lines 49-58, heading 'اختبار روحي')): Close your eyelids and look at what you see. If you say 'I see nothing then', you are wrong: you do see, but the darkness of existence is so close to your inner sight that you do not find it. If you want to find it and see it in front of you with your eyelids closed, reduce something of your existence or put some of it at a distance; the way to reduce it is striving, which means spending effort to repel or kill what is other: existence, the soul and Satan. (Both OCRs are noisy here; the sense is secure from the two together.)
  - passage (method; Meier sec. 6 (Meier file lines 322-324; Zaydan file lines 121-124 and 161-163)): The third way is the way of Junayd, eight conditions: constant ablution, constant fasting, constant silence, constant seclusion, constant dhikr (la ilaha illa'llah), constant binding of the heart to the shaykh and drawing the knowledge of visionary events from him by letting his direction replace one's own, constant rejection of stray thoughts, and constant giving up of objection to God. The end of the last clause is broken in the OCR ('... or seeking refuge from a fire'); the editor reads it as not asking for paradise or refuge from hell.
  - passage (stages; Second absorption, Meier sec. 49 (Zaydan file lines 1290-1299; Meier file lines 1142-1146)): After the stage of sounds, dhikr opens a door at the crown of the head, a round opening like a circle, and from above there descends on him darkness, then fire, then green: the darkness of existence, the fire of dhikr, the green of the heart. The door opens at the top first because dhikr is a good word that rises.
  - passage (development; Meier sec. 13-15 (Zaydan file lines 384-419; Meier file lines 485-512)): When you see before you a wide open space with clear air above it and, at the limit of sight, colours such as green, red, yellow and blue, you are crossing that air toward those colours, and the colours are colours of states. Green is the sign of the heart's life; the colour of clear fire is the life of spiritual resolve [همة], which means power; a murky colour is the fire of hardship in the struggle with the soul; blue is the life of the soul; yellow is weakness. One colour holding means composure; colours mixing is instability; green holding steady is settledness. Green is the last colour to remain, and from it the flashes and lightnings shine; its clear part is the light of the Real, its murk the darkness of existence.
  - passage (mode; Chapter on the stars and suns seen in trance (Meier file lines 3203-3207; Zaydan file lines 3856-3860)): This has beginnings and ends. At first something like a point appears in trance, then it grows and becomes stars. It may appear when he is not absent but has his eyelids closed; then it increases and these stars appear to the wayfarer in the light of day with his eyelids not closed, and praise comes to his tongue unbidden.
  - passage (caution; Meier sec. 8 (Zaydan file lines 237-248, heading 'نيران الذكر'; Meier file lines 395-402)): The fires of dhikr are clear, fast-moving and rise upward; Satan's fire is murky, smoky, dark and slow. They are also told apart by state: if the wayfarer is under a great weight, tight in the chest, dhikr hard for him and his limbs as if crushed by stones while he sees a dark fire, it is Satan's; if he is light, dignified, open-chested and at rest and sees a clear rising fire, as one sees fire in dry wood, these are the fires of dhikr in the space of the chest.
- new leads: Majd al-Din Baghdadi, Tuhfat al-barara, and his Persian letter to Sharaf al-Din Balkhi (cited in the editors' notes as parallel to passages here) | Ammar al-Bidlisi, Kubra's shaykh, whose retreat advice Kubra quotes; his own treatises were not seen in this run

## [suf-razi-mirsad-al-ibad] Mirsad al-'ibad min al-mabda' ila'l-ma'ad (The Path of God's Bondsmen from Origin to Return), Part 3, chs. 12-18: dhikr, khalwa, visions, the witnessing of lights
Najm al-Din Razi, known as Daya (d. 654/1256); 1223 (first recension), 1230 (second); editions 1933 and 1973; classical_text; rights public_domain; access partial_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 4, confidence medium (Persian OCR noisy with interleaved apparatus; colour list partly garbled after yellow (red/green/white not legible in that passage); method and stages read directly in source.)
- mode: perceptual. Lights are 'witnessed' (mushahada) by the heart in the dark cell with eyes shut, in a fixed sequence of forms and colours; the sun-like light is so strong 'the gaze cannot master its rays'. Razi says form and colour are imagination's clothing of the light from behind veils; when light comes out of the veils imagination has no hold and it is seen without colour, form or place. So: perceptual, closed-eye, but seen by the heart's eye; form layer explicitly imaginal.
- eyes: closed; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET induction: Lights arise as a consequence of method: the dhikr la ilaha illa'llah, received by talqin from a shaykh, struck from navel into the heart, gradually polishes the heart-mirror; 'in proportion as polish increases lights grow stronger and more'. Ch.15 khalwa: forty days, alone in a small dark clean cell facing qibla, cross-legged, hands on thighs, eyes closed, leaving only for ablution, need and prayer, constant fasting, silence, negation of thoughts, heart tied to the shaykh. No separate advice for one who sees nothing; time scale implied by the forty-day arba'in; lights are graded by the heart's purity rather than forced.
- techniques: sound_or_mantra, concentration_nonvisual, darkness_exposure, sensory_restriction, fasting_or_austerity, posture_or_body_lock, devotion_or_transmission, eye_pressure_or_sense_closure; phenomena: lightning_flashes, sparks_or_points, lamp_or_flame, stars, moon_disc, sun_disc, blue, gold_yellow, red, white, black_light, all_pervading_light
- conditions: posture: Cross-legged (murabba') facing qibla, hands on thighs; cross-legged otherwise disapproved except during dhikr.; gaze_or_eye_position: Eyes closed (chashm faraham/bar ham kunad) during dhikr.; setting: Small, dark, empty, clean room; incense optional.; duration_and_schedule: Forty-day khalwa (arba'in); leave only for ablution, need, prayer.; prerequisites: Repentance, constant ablution, dhikr received from a shaykh, heart bound to the shaykh.; other: Continuous fasting, silence, negation of passing thoughts; ablution itself said to carry light.
- stages: buruq, lawami', lawa'ih: Flashes that leap and vanish; gleams that follow one another and pause briefly; glints like sunlight reflected off a mirror that stay a little then go behind the veil. Mostly from dhikr, ablution and prayer. -> lamp, candle, torch, kindled fires: Light borrowed from the shaykh's walaya, the Prophet, the Qur'an or faith; the heart itself as a lamp lit to that measure (niche of the Light verse). -> stars, moon, sun: Spirit's light on the heart's sky in proportion to polish: star-sized when the heart is star-pure; full or partial moon by its clarity; sun when the heart receives spirit's light, sometimes a thousand times brighter. Moon and sun together = heart and spirit. -> lights without locus: Out of the veils: colourless, formless, placeless; the absolute light. -> colours by station: Blue from spirit/dhikr light mixed with the nafs's darkness (blue Sufi novice robes mark this); yellow when spirit prevails; a further colour when darkness [OCR garbled]; sun-like with rays when the heart is wholly clear; then a light like the sun in a clear mirror that the eye cannot bear. -> jalal lights: Lights of the attributes of Majesty burn rather than illumine; sometimes a purely dark light (nur-i zulmani), illustrated by hell fired red, white, then black.
- with the light: Read the light as a gauge of the heart's polish and its source; do not take formed or coloured light as God (Abraham's star-moon-sun, 'I love not the setters'); continue dhikr until light is formless.
- obstacles/fixes: Frightening images and sounds in khalwa: call the shaykh's name and seek his help (himma). | Passing thoughts imprint the heart and block the lights: negate every thought with la ilaha. | Formed or coloured light is still behind veils of imagination: keep polishing.
- cautions: Every form and colour perceived is the veil of human attributes worked by imagination; the Real is beyond form and setting. | Abraham's error: taking reflections in the station of talwin for the Lord.
- safety flags: fasting, prolonged_darkness, transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: Lights are reflections of spirit, of dhikr, of the shaykh, Qur'an and divine attributes on the polished heart-mirror; their forms and colours are imagination's clothing, and the pure light is formless.
- practice: Repent, keep ablution, and take the formula la ilaha illa'llah from a shaykh. Enter a small, dark, clean, empty cell for forty days, leaving only for ablution, need and prayer; fast continuously and keep silence. Sit cross-legged facing qibla, hands on thighs, eyes closed, heart present, and strike the formula with force: la ilaha drawn up from the navel, negating every thought, illa'llah driven down into the heart. Keep the heart bound to the shaykh; if fearful images or sounds come, invoke him. As the heart is polished, expect first flashes, gleams and mirror-like glints; then lamps, candles, torches and fires; then stars, moon and sun in proportion to purity; finally lights without place. Colours track station: blue while spirit's light mixes with the nafs's darkness, yellow when spirit prevails, sun-like rays when the heart is clear. Treat all forms and colours as veils and go on until the light is colourless.
  - passage (method; Part 3 ch.13, source lines 186-194): While saying dhikr, place the hands on the thighs, make the heart present and close the eyes.
  - passage (method; Part 3 ch.15, source lines 578-581): In khalwa leave only for ablution, need and prayer; the room must be dark and small.
  - passage (onset; Part 3 ch.17, source lines c.1271-1279): The more the polish increases, the stronger and more numerous the lights; after flashes come lamps, candles, torches and fires.
  - passage (mode; Part 3 ch.17, source lines c.1300-1305): When lights come fully out of the veils imagination has no hold; colours vanish and light is witnessed without colour, form or shape.
  - passage (stages; Part 3 ch.17, colours, source lines c.1600-1625): Blue light comes from the mixing of spirit's or dhikr's light with the nafs's darkness; yellow when spirit prevails.

## [suf-rinn-marabouts-et-khouan-1884] Marabouts et Khouan: etude sur l'Islam en Algerie, ch. XX (Khelouatya): extracts from the order's books after Shaykh al-Sanusi, on dhikr, khalwa visions and the seventy thousand lights
Louis Rinn, translating Muhammad b. 'Ali al-Sanusi (d. 1859) on the Khalwatiyya; also quoting 'Abd al-Wahhab al-Sha'rani (d. 1565); 1884; secondary_conduit; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 3, confidence high (Relevant chapter read in full; the explanatory table pp. 300-301 is OCR-garbled and was not read. French via a 19th-c. translation of al-Sanusi.)
- mode: ambiguous. Al-Sanusi's extract distinguishes a 'vision' (seen in dream) from a 'perception' (ce qui frappe les sens) in the somnolent state between sleep and waking, and from 'apparitions' in waking; the 70,000 lights are 'perceived' with named colours. It never states eyes open/closed or locates them in the visual field, so sensory character is asserted but not described.
- eyes: unspecified; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET development_only: Nothing for the one who sees nothing beyond the regimen itself: apparitions 'can strike the adept only in solitude and only after long practices of piety'. Each of ten Divine Names is taken up only after the shaykh transmits the 'revelations' of the previous one. Seeing requires self-abnegation; the lights are 'entirely subject to the will of God'; most affiliates, even the most fervent, never perceive them (the Companions are cited). Sha'rani (quoted by Rinn) holds sanctity is a gift, not acquired, and that lights from fasting/sleeplessness/isolation are delusions. No time scale given.
- techniques: sound_or_mantra, movement, posture_or_body_lock, sensory_restriction, fasting_or_austerity, devotion_or_transmission; phenomena: white, blue, red, gold_yellow, green, colour_fields, smoke_mist_cloud, figures_or_forms, all_pervading_light
- conditions: posture: Seated cross-legged; in circle the head is swung obliquely right into the circle then back left outward on 'Huwa' (Lui).; setting: Solitude/khalwa; group circle for collective dhikr; black clothing.; duration_and_schedule: La ilaha illa'llah 'for a certain time', mouth carried from right shoulder to under the left breast; then ten Names in fixed order, each followed by 100 Fatiha and 100 salat on the Prophet; after all ten, 100 Kawthar.; prerequisites: Shaykh's permission to move to each next Name; purified heart; for red lights, diet stripped of the foods one most craves.; other: Somnolent state between sleep and waking named as locus of 'perceptions'.
- stages: lumière des ablutions et des prières: First light, from ritual purity and prayer; then the 'light of the demon' with that of honours; then truth appears as coral, palm, horses, one's own form, the shaykh's form (said to have killed many). -> 1 humanité: 10,000 dull lights intermingling; jinn seen; easy to pass. -> 2 extase passionnée: Heart sanctified; 10,000 light-blue lights. -> 3 extase du coeur: Hell seen; 10,000 lights red as pure flame; if food not stripped of favourites they appear smoke-dulled, and one must go no further. -> 4 extase de l'âme immatérielle: Strong yellow lights; souls of prophets and saints. -> 5 extase mystérieuse: Brilliant white; angels. -> 6 extase d'obsession: Colour of clear mirrors; rapture called al-Khadir; the Prophet seen. -> 7 béatitude: Green and white, passing through gem colours to a clear tint, then a colour without likeness spread through the universe; worldly things vanish.
- with the light: Submit visions and perceptions to the shaykh for interpretation; waking apparitions are not to be interpreted like dreams; at smoke-dulled red lights stop; do not trust lights over the prescribed litanies.
- obstacles/fixes: Smoke-dulled red lights: purify diet of craved foods; else go no further. | Being dazzled: few visionaries avoid it, so shaykhs do not always follow what revelations seem to say.
- cautions: Visions of forms 'caused the death of many'. | All revelations except that of the divine attributes are a slippery slope; the devil shows a false throne between heaven and earth. | Sha'rani: exhaustion by abstinence, silence, insomnia and isolation yields phantoms, lights or darkness, hideous images; one recluse starved to death. | Supernatural lights cannot replace the wird (Shadhili extract, p. 258).
- safety flags: fasting, sleep_deprivation, transmission_required_by_tradition, tradition_warns_against_seeking
- interpretation: Lights are degrees of the soul's ascent (seven 'ramparts', 70,000 lights), gifts subject to God's will, reachable by struggle and asceticism in any religion, except the unveiling of the divine attributes reserved to followers of the Prophet.
- practice: Under a Khalwati shaykh, sit cross-legged and repeat la ilaha illa'llah, swinging the mouth from over the right shoulder to below the left breast. Then take up ten Names in order (Huwa, Haqq, Hayy, Qahhar, Wahhab, Razzaq, Fattah, Wahid, Ahad, Samad), each followed by a hundred Fatihas and a hundred blessings on the Prophet, moving to the next Name only when the shaykh conveys that its 'revelations' have come. Work in solitude over long periods. The first lights are those of ablution and prayer; expect deceptive lights and forms and report everything to the shaykh. A graded ladder of coloured lights follows (dull, light blue, flame red, yellow, white, mirror-clear, green-white). Diet must be stripped of craved foods for the red lights; if they appear smoky, stop. Lights are not in the practitioner's control and most never see them; the litanies take precedence over any light.
  - passage (onset; Ch. XX, p. 297, source lines 14830-14840): Apparitions come only in solitude after long devotions; first the light of ablutions and prayers, then the demon's light, then forms.
  - passage (mode; Ch. XX, p. 296, source lines 14790-14800): A vision is seen in dream; a perception strikes the senses in somnolence between sleep and waking; waking apparitions are not interpreted like dreams.
  - passage (method; Ch. XX, pp. 295-296, source lines 14715-14735): Posture and dhikr movement; ten Names each begun only after the shaykh transmits the revelations of the previous one.
  - passage (stages; Ch. XX, p. 298, source lines 14880-14890): Red lights of the third degree require a diet free of most-craved foods; otherwise smoky, and one must stop.
  - passage (caution; Ch. XX, p. 302, source lines 15385-15420): Luminous apparitions are subject to God's will; require self-abnegation; most affiliates never see them; seeking them has led many astray or to death.
  - passage (caution; Ch. XX, p. 294, source lines 14650-14660 (Sha'rani quoted by Rinn)): Sha'rani: those exhausted by fasting, silence, insomnia and isolation see phantoms, lights or darkness and vile images; sanctity is God's gift.
- new leads: Al-Sanusi's source text (al-Salsabil al-ma'in) for the Arabic of the 70,000 lights and the table on pp. 300-301 (OCR unreadable)

## [suf-suhrawardi-hikmat-al-ishraq-lights] Hikmat al-ishraq (The Philosophy of Illumination), Part 2, Discourse 5: 'On the states of the wayfarers' (the fifteen kinds of light) and the author's Testament
Shihab al-Din Yahya al-Suhrawardi (d. 587/1191); 1186; Corbin's edition 1952; this printing undated in metadata; classical_text; rights public_domain; access partial_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 3, confidence medium (Arabic OCR with Corbin's bracketed variants; clauses of the light list are translated from the digest and the search agent's summary.)
- mode: perceptual. One light is said to appear 'as witnessing and seeing, more evident than the sun'; others come with bodily sensations (hot water over the head, numbness in the brain, a grip on the head) and thunder-like sound. He also describes suspended images (muthul mu'allaqa) seen by the self, distinct from the brain, so these are not mind's-eye pictures. Eyes open or closed are not specified.
- eyes: unspecified; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: Para. 276 lists aids that lead to these states: closeness to God, little food, vigil, supplication, refining the inner self with subtle thoughts, grasping signs in creation, constant dhikr to God's majesty, sincerity toward the Light of Lights, singing God's remembrance, reading revealed scripture, promptness in return to the Lord. In the Testament, forty days of discipline: no meat, little food, devoted to contemplation of God's light, before studying the book. A flashing light 'comes to beginners', so some light is expected early. No time scale except the forty days.
- techniques: fasting_or_austerity, sound_or_mantra, sensory_restriction, devotion_or_transmission, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena, concentration_nonvisual; phenomena: lightning_flashes, sun_disc, body_or_objects_luminous, all_pervading_light, figures_or_forms
- conditions: setting: Retreat of forty days; sanctuaries of prayer.; duration_and_schedule: Forty days before taking up the book; vigil and reduced food generally.; prerequisites: Training in the Peripatetic way and a love of God's light; guidance of the 'keeper of the book' for the secret.
- stages: flashing light for beginners: A pleasant light that gleams and folds away like a lightning flash. -> great flash: A greater, fearful flash, possibly with thunder-like sound or roar in the brain. -> hot-water light: A pleasant light arriving like hot water poured on the head. -> fixed light: A long-lasting forceful light with numbness in the brain. -> love light: A very pleasant light with sweet joy, moved by love. -> burning light: A light that burns, moved by the power of might. -> sun-surpassing flash: A light in a great snatch, seen 'more evident than the sun', in overwhelming delight. -> hair-hanging light: A brilliant light that seems attached to the hair of the head. -> grip lights: Lights with a grasp that pulls the head or is fixed in the brain. -> body-clothing light: A light that shines from the soul over the whole spirit, so that the body seems clothed in it. -> heavy and forceful lights: A light with unbearable weight; a light with force that nearly dislocates the joints.
- with the light: Treat these as the attainments of the intermediate: reflections of the governing light on the body. Beyond them is the great 'habit of death', passing into the world of light. Lights tinged with might or love are useful for corresponding effects.
- obstacles/fixes: Fearsome flashes and sounds may frighten beginners or come from thought and imagination; the text notes some burning lights bring dreadful drums and horns for the beginner.
- cautions: Keep the book from those who are not fit; no one should expect to learn its secrets without the keeper of the book. | Lights are not the goal: the highest state is separation of the governing light from darkness.
- safety flags: fasting, sleep_deprivation
- interpretation: The lights are illuminations on the governing light, reflected into the body; they are way-stations on the path to the Light of Lights.
- practice: Sources of the lights, as Suhrawardi lists them: nearness to God by reduced food, vigil and supplication; refining the inner self with subtle thoughts; reading signs in the world as pointers to God; constant dhikr of God's majesty; sincerity toward the Light of Lights; reading revealed books. Before studying his book, keep forty days of discipline: no meat, little food, contemplate God's light. The lights that may come: first a pleasant flash for beginners; then stronger flashes with a thunder-like noise; a light like hot water over the head; a lasting one with numbness; a sweet light of love; a burning light; a flash brighter than the sun; a light seemingly hanging from the hair; lights that grip the head; a light clothing the body. These are the goals of the intermediate, not of the end.
  - passage (stages; Para. 272 (Corbin 272), source lines 187-208): The brethren of detachment receive lights of many kinds; the list begins with a pleasant flashing light for beginners.
  - passage (mode; Para. 272, source lines 195-196): One light appears as witnessing and seeing, more evident than the sun, in overwhelming delight.
  - passage (method; Para. 276, source lines 243-251): Reduced food, vigil and supplication, with constant dhikr and sincerity toward the Light of Lights, lead to these states.
  - passage (onset; Testament, source lines 269-275): Before starting the book, forty days of discipline without meat, eating little, devoted to contemplation of God's light.

## [suf-tawakkul-beg-initiation-by-mulla-shah] Tawakkul Beg's account of his initiation by Mulla Shah (from the Nuskha-i ahwal-i shahi), in Macdonald, Lecture VII
Tawakkul Beg Kulali (17th c.), disciple of Mulla Shah Badakhshi (d. 1661); 17th century; English 1909; practitioner_testimony; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 5, practicality 4, confidence medium (Translated from von Kremer's French rendering by Macdonald; the later narrative was read by sed after the digest ended mid-story.)
- mode: imaginal_crossing. The first image, with eyes bandaged, is a reproduction of the cell and the master 'within me', a self-generated image told to him to 'reproduce his own image'. He then sees the master both with the physical eye and with spiritual sight and cries that either way 'it is you that I see'; the dazzling figure speaks 'in the voice of the heart' and is heard 'with my spiritual ear'; later figures pass 'before my inner sight'. The report treats the image as inner perception that becomes as vivid as ordinary sight, but not as an external light in the visual field.
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: not_applicable
- ONSET both: For someone seeing nothing: recite sura 112 a hundred thousand times; sit whole nights facing Mecca and concentrate on the heart while the master and adepts direct their attention (tawajjuh) at you. Three nights, nothing; on the fourth, toward dawn, 'some little light and clearness' in the heart, without colour or form. The master then bandages the eyes, tells the novice to reproduce his image and concentrate on the heart; the heart 'opens' immediately 'by the divine favor and by the spiritual assistance of the shaykh'. Transmission by the teacher is essential to the account.
- techniques: concentration_nonvisual, visualization_crossing, sound_or_mantra, devotion_or_transmission, sensory_restriction, sleep_threshold; phenomena: figures_or_forms, colour_fields, light_in_the_room
- conditions: posture: Sitting through the night with the face turned toward Mecca.; gaze_or_eye_position: Eyes bandaged by the master for the opening; later alternating bandaged and uncovered.; setting: In the master's cell, nights, with attention directed by the master and two adepts.; duration_and_schedule: Four nights before any light; then three months of figures; after a year, the doctrine of absolute reality.; prerequisites: A master who directs his attention; the novice's own recitation of sura 112 one hundred thousand times.
- stages: light and clearness: On the fourth night, a little light and clearness in the heart, with neither colour nor form. -> heart opens: With eyes bandaged and the master's image reproduced, an overturned cup is set upright, with unbounded happiness; a faithful reproduction of the cell and the master within. -> dazzling figure: A dazzling figure of 'Abd al-Qadir al-Jilani approaches and speaks to the heart. -> sphere of colours and images: For three months, figures of the Prophet, Companions, angels and saints pass before the inner sight. -> sphere where all colour is effaced: After three months the colourless sphere opens and all images vanish.
