  - passage (onset; Summary of Practical Directions, rule 2, source lines 1459-1539): Usually nothing is seen at first; he prescribes calm, patience and perseverance.
  - passage (method; Summary of Practical Directions, rule 3, source lines 1459-1539): Gaze steadily and calmly for ten minutes at the first sitting, lengthening gradually.

## [wes-randolph-seership] Seership! The Magnetic Mirror: A Practical Guide for Those Who Aspire to Clairvoyance-Absolute
Paschal Beverly Randolph; 1870; teacher_manual; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 4, confidence medium (Digest only; the 'horse-shoe magnet operation' and the Part I numbered rules were not visible.)
- mode: perceptual. Sparks, flashes, streaks of quick and lingering light, and 'phosphor clouds' are said to be seen 'before the face' in a dark room, and clouds are seen moving on the mirror's face; a correspondent reports a pale golden misty light cut by flashes seen with a bandaged, shut eye. Randolph also says true vision occurs in 'interior repose' when outward light is separated, and that sparks may resolve into forms, so his seen light shades into imaginal seeing.
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: unclear
- ONSET both: If after a few minutes in a quite dark room sparks, flashes, streaks or phosphor clouds appear, the person can become clairvoyant by continuance and repetition. To someone who sees nothing: a steady-gaze test (a speck on the wall six minutes), rapid forcible breathing for ninety seconds, a horse-shoe magnet operation lying down, mirror-gazing, and patience. Persons already seeing fiery sparks that glitter and vanish into blank void are called splendid seers. Not forced: 'Mystery never opens her dark doors to the impatient seeker'; he admits some try long without success.
- techniques: darkness_exposure, gazing_fixed_point, breath_control, scrying_surface, sensory_restriction, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena, eye_pressure_or_sense_closure; phenomena: sparks_or_points, lightning_flashes, smoke_mist_cloud, gold_yellow, blue, figures_or_forms, light_in_the_room
- conditions: posture: lying down for the magnet and breath operation; gaze_or_eye_position: steady look at a speck on the wall for six minutes; mirror gazing; a bandage with thicknesses of paper over eyes and forehead at night in one correspondent's practice; setting: quite dark room (said to be twenty times more productive than a lit one); moonlight next best, starlight better still; duration_and_schedule: a few minutes for sparks to show; sittings repeated; one correspondent waited twenty minutes before the first appearance and three weeks before the eye-form; prerequisites: health, cleanliness, freedom from doubt, irritability and impatience; steady will, lung power; other: magnetic mirrors of black polished glass used afterward
- stages: sparks and phosphor clouds: Sparks, flashes and streaks of quick and lingering light, or phosphor clouds floating before the face. -> clouds on the mirror: The first sign in mirror work: curious clouds moving on the glass, then phantoramas. -> forms: The clouds and sparks may resolve into forms of departed friends; a correspondent saw a golden misty light, a moving spot that became an eye with blue pupil and gold ring.
- with the light: Continue and repeat the sitting; watch the sparks and clouds until they resolve into forms; keep patience and moral steadiness.
- obstacles/fixes: Nothing appears: continue; some are not susceptible | Impatience, doubt, irritability, ill health defeat the power | Artificial or solar light lowers results
- cautions: Under lust, hatred, avarice or wrong no man can enter | Spiritual trance mediumship is mostly delusion; true seership is experience, not hearsay | Rapid forcible breathing for ninety seconds is described as making the subject dizzy, repeated it is almost certain to produce coma
- safety flags: prolonged_darkness
- interpretation: Sparks and flashes are 'instantaneous penetrations' of the veil between the outer world of dark and cold and the inner realm of light and fire; true clairvoyance is the lengthened uplifting of that veil.
- practice: Sit or lie in a quite dark room, ideally with moonlight or starlight as the next best condition. Look steadily at a speck on the wall for six minutes, or breathe rapidly and forcibly for ninety seconds, then lie still. After a few minutes watch for sparks, flashes, streaks or phosphor clouds before the face. If they come, he says continuance and repetition will make you clairvoyant, and the clouds may resolve into forms. Mirror gazing in a dark chamber is the second method; the first sign there is clouds passing over the glass. One correspondent wore a paper-padded bandage over the eyes at night and after weeks saw a golden misty light and a moving eye-shaped spot. Stay patient, clean and calm; impatience closes the door.
  - passage (onset; Interior Vision, tests of susceptibility, source lines 642-678): If these lights appear within a few minutes in the dark, the person can become clairvoyant by continuance and repetition.
  - passage (method; Interior Vision, source lines 642-678): Dark chamber first, moonlight next, then starlight, as the best conditions.
  - passage (interpretation; Interior Vision, what is clairvoyance, source lines 1487-1544): The sparks we see at night are instantaneous penetrations of the veil to the inner realm of light.
  - passage (testimony; Letter from Ohio correspondent, source lines 3919-3956): With a bandaged eye and a mirror, the correspondent saw a pale golden misty light, then a moving gold spot that formed an eye.
  - passage (onset; Letter from Ohio correspondent, source lines 3919-3956): After twenty minutes of nothing, the first appearance showed itself suddenly on the mirror disk.

## [wes-reichenbach-researches] Researches on Magnetism, Electricity, Heat, Light, Crystallization, and Chemical Attraction, in their Relations to the Vital Force (Parts I and II)
Karl, Baron von Reichenbach; German 1845-49; English 1850; ethnographic_report; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep_marginal; relevance 2, practicality 2, confidence low (Digest only; light is stimulus-bound to physical objects and the dark-adaptation durations were not confirmed.)
- mode: perceptual. Sensitive subjects in a darkened room report flames, vapour, rays and glows at magnet poles and crystals, seen with colour, some even through closed lids. The lights are tied to physical objects and are not reported to arise without them, but Reichenbach insists they are seen, not imagined, and compares many independent observers.
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: no
- ONSET induction: The only induction in the digest is dark adaptation: the subject enters a darkened room and stays 'till her eyes were accustomed to the darkness' before the light is seen. Subjects are usually highly sensitive, often ill. The search agent's summary says moderately sensitive persons need about two hours in total darkness; that was not seen in the digest. No claim about whether light can be forced.
- techniques: darkness_exposure, light_source_gazing; phenomena: lamp_or_flame, smoke_mist_cloud, colour_fields, blue, red, white, sparks_or_points, rainbow
- conditions: gaze_or_eye_position: open eyes toward the object; some see the light with closed lids at a certain distance; setting: completely darkened room, eyes accustomed to the dark; duration_and_schedule: time to adapt to the dark (two hours per the search agent, unconfirmed); prerequisites: 'sensitive' constitution; other: a distance is needed: the flame vanishes when approached closely
- stages: dark-adapted perception: Greyish feebly luminous flames over the poles appear first, then increase in intensity and develop colours; blue on the north pole, reddish yellow on the south.
- with the light: Observation only; he records colours, sizes and forms for comparison between observers.
- obstacles/fixes: Flame is distinctly seen only at a certain distance: step back from the object
- cautions: Strong light from crystals became too intense to bear and was removed because of overexcitement
- safety flags: prolonged_darkness
- interpretation: A new imponderable 'odylic' force, shown as feeble light, emanating from magnets, crystals, metals and the human hand.
- practice: This is a laboratory record, not a method. Reichenbach's subjects enter a room made as dark as possible and wait until the eyes are accustomed to the dark. Then a magnet or crystal is shown at a distance and they report a flame, vapour or glow over the poles, with colour. Flames show best at some distance and vanish when approached. Some also detect the light through closed lids, pointing to where it comes from but not seeing its form. Highly sensitive persons see quickly; the search agent says others need up to two hours in total darkness. Nothing here trains light that persists without the physical object.
  - passage (method; Part I, §55, source lines 4331-4463): The patient waits in the darkened room until her eyes are accustomed to it before the crystal is shown.
  - passage (testimony; §206, source lines 8869-8906): Mile. Reichel saw metals in the dark with a delicate vaporous flame that moved with air, breath or the hand.
  - passage (mode; §600, source lines 22273-22310): Several sensitives saw the odylic light with closed lids and could say where it came from.
  - passage (stages; §§ on electro-magnet flames, source lines 17442-17479): First the glow is perceived, then after a minute or two the flames appear, grey and faint at first, then brighter and coloured.

## [wes-sepharial-how-to-read-the-crystal] How to Read the Crystal; or, Crystal and Seer
Sepharial (Walter Gorn Old); c. 1922 (undated in the e-text); teacher_manual; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 4, practicality 5, confidence high
- mode: perceptual. He describes the vision as 'a vivid picture affecting the focus and retina of the eye', with pupils expanding and contracting and sparkling specks in the milky field, and says the visions are 'in the soul of the seer', the crystal acting through the optic nerves. The second kind is an 'apperception rather than a perception'. So most is claimed as seen, with some imaginal impression.
- eyes: open; stimulus independence: no
- ONSET both: For one who sees nothing: regular short sittings of fifteen minutes, daily, at the same hour and place; nothing may happen for the first few sittings or many; the faculty 'in a few days, weeks, or months' may flower. Appended directions say those seemingly insensitive can develop it by frequent trials of fifteen to twenty minutes. If the quest is unsuccessful after a period of earnest trial it is warrant that the faculty is not among one's powers. Prerequisites: truthfulness, resignation, no selfish desire. Some are 'born rather than made'.
- techniques: scrying_surface, gazing_fixed_point, concentration_visual_object, darkness_exposure; phenomena: smoke_mist_cloud, sparks_or_points, gold_yellow, blue, colour_fields, red, green, black_light, figures_or_forms
- conditions: posture: sit comfortably with back to the light, sphere held in the palm on the lap, or on a stand against a black velvet screen; gaze_or_eye_position: steady gazing in complete silence; setting: moderately warm, shady room, not actually dark, light enough to just read average print; no direct rays on the crystal; others at arm's length and silent; duration_and_schedule: fifteen minutes at first, daily, same hour and place; fifteen to twenty minutes in the directions; prerequisites: love of truth, self-possession, freedom from selfish desire; other: keep the crystal in a black cover in the dark; low monotone questions only
- stages: cloud over: The crystal becomes dull, then suffused with milky clouds with specks of light like gold dust in sunlight; focus inconstant; the crystal may vanish in a haze and return. -> azure expanse: Clouds and dancing starlights suddenly vanish; a bright azure expanse like a summer sky fills the field; a spasm-like sensation in the brain, tightening of the scalp, a caught breath. -> vision: The first vision stands clear and life-like against the azure screen. -> sphere directions: Sphere ceases to reflect and turns milky; a clouded colour follows (generally red, then green), turning to blackness that rolls away like a curtain to disclose pictures.
- with the light: Persevere through the cloud stage; stay self-possessed at the azure moment so as not to be startled out of the state.
- obstacles/fixes: Nothing happens for many sittings: keep regular, patient and self-controlled | Being startled into full waking consciousness at the moment of vision: self-possession and confidence | Distraction: silence, shade, no reflections
- cautions: Ceremonial magic with the crystal is 'highly dangerous' | A selfish or self-seeking spirit will distort or shut the visions | Excess of joy or fear at the vision is fatal to its continuance
- interpretation: The visions are in the soul of the seer; the crystal's odylic substance responds to the seer's nervo-vital emanations and acts on the brain centres through the optic nerves.
- practice: Sit with your back to a north or shaded light, with a good unblemished crystal or sphere on a stand against black velvet so no reflections or direct rays fall on it. The room should be shady but light enough to read by. Gaze steadily and silently for fifteen minutes, daily at the same hour and place. The first signs are that the crystal dulls, fills with milky clouds with sparkling specks like gold dust, and may vanish in haze while your eyes lose focus. This may be all that happens for a few sittings or many. Then the clouds and starlights vanish, a bright azure sky fills the field, you feel a stir in the head and the scalp tightens, and the vision appears on the blue. Stay calm at that moment so you are not startled out of it.
  - passage (stages; Ch. IV The Vision, source lines 441-561): In early development the crystal clouds with milky haze and sparkling specks of light like gold dust.
  - passage (stages; Ch. IV, source lines 441-561): Sooner or later the clouds vanish and a bright azure expanse fills the field, on which the vision appears.
  - passage (onset; Ch. III, source lines 441-561 (opening)): The faculty of pure vision is latent; in days, weeks or months it may flower.
  - passage (onset; Ch. VIII, source lines 1195-1263): Seemingly insensitive people can develop the faculty by frequent short trials.
  - passage (caution; Ch. IV, source lines 441-561): At the moment of vision a start can throw the seer back; self-possession is needed.

## [wes-steiner-way-of-initiation] The Way of Initiation; or, How to Attain Knowledge of the Higher Worlds (with its sequel, Initiation and Its Results)
Rudolf Steiner; German articles 1904-05; English 1908 and 1909; teacher_manual; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 3, practicality 4, confidence medium (Digest only; the 'Probation' exercises and mode of seeing were not visible.)
- mode: ambiguous. The luminous cloud and flame around a physical seed are said to be perceived by 'spiritualized vision' with 'spiritual eyes', 'colors of a spiritual kind which are discerned', not by the physical eye. He anticipates the objection 'what is the value of such visions or hallucinations?' and insists they differ from phantasy, but gives no account of where in the visual field they are located.
- eyes: open; stimulus independence: unclear
- ONSET induction: An explicit method for a beginner: contemplate crystal, plant and animal with set thoughts and feeling; then a real seed with the thought that the invisible plant force lies in it, felt intensely. 'Possibly not until after numerous attempts' an inward force makes itself felt and the seed appears in a small luminous cloud. Patience, moral development, reverence and health are prerequisites; an experienced guide is advised beyond this point; the fire of Kundalini 'may only form the subject of actual occult training'.
- techniques: concentration_visual_object, attention_to_spontaneous_phenomena, devotion_or_transmission, energy_work; phenomena: smoke_mist_cloud, lamp_or_flame, colour_fields, violet, blue, green, gold_yellow
- conditions: gaze_or_eye_position: observe the object with fixed attention; setting: calmness of mind, free of disturbing influence; duration_and_schedule: repeated over many attempts; each thought given time to become embedded in the soul; prerequisites: probation exercises of reverence; moral strength and inner purity; healthy sober judgment; a guide for further steps; other: no other thoughts or emotions to be mixed in
- stages: seed in a luminous cloud: The seed appears enclosed in a small luminous cloud perceived as a flame, lilac at the centre, blue at the edges. -> mature plant: After the withering-and-seed exercise, a larger flame, greenish at the centre and tinged with yellow at the outer edge. -> lotus organs lucent: In the sequel the astral lotus-flowers first become lucent and later revolve; clairvoyance begins then.
- with the light: Do not luxuriate in it; keep a clear head and conscious control, and repeat for the subsequent plant exercise.
- obstacles/fixes: Probably the experiment fails at first: repeat with patience | Doubt that the flame is mere illusion: keep distinguishing reality from phantasy with sober self-examination | Emotions fade with the object: practise until they arise without it
- cautions: Never surrender fully conscious control of oneself; avoid reveries | Do not go beyond the elementary stage without an experienced guide | Experimenting in the dark is strongly discouraged by true occultism | Steiner says crystal-gazing is a distortion of the true contemplation of crystals
- safety flags: tradition_warns_against_seeking, transmission_required_by_tradition
- interpretation: Spiritual colours and flames are the invisible forces of life and form behind objects, perceived by spiritual eyes (organs formed by cultivated feeling and thought).
- practice: First cultivate reverence and moral steadiness. Then contemplate a transparent crystal, a plant and an animal with fixed attention and set thoughts, until distinct feelings arise from each and later arise without the object. Next place a real seed before you and think intensely, in calm, that the invisible future plant lies in it, feeling that thought deeply. Repeat. Possibly only after numerous attempts, the seed appears wrapped in a small luminous cloud, a flame lilac at the centre and blue at the edge. Follow with a full-grown plant and the thought of its decay and seeds, giving a greenish flame tinged yellow. Keep sober control throughout and seek a guide for further steps.
  - passage (method; Way of Initiation, V Enlightenment, source lines 2320-2377): After the seed exercise, the seed appears in a luminous cloud perceived as a flame by spiritualized vision.
  - passage (onset; Way of Initiation, V Enlightenment, source lines 2320-2377): Success may take numerous attempts; an inward force then makes itself felt.
  - passage (mode; Way of Initiation, V Enlightenment, source lines 2106-2209): The colours are of a spiritual kind, discerned by clairvoyant organs formed by the exercise.
  - passage (caution; Way of Initiation, source lines 2320-2377): The student must keep full conscious control and avoid reveries.
  - passage (stages; Initiation and Its Results, source lines 496-534): With practice the astral lotus-flowers first become lucent and later revolve, at which point clairvoyance starts.

## [wes-thomas-crystal-gazing] Crystal Gazing: Its History and Practice, with a Discussion of the Evidence for Telepathic Scrying
Northcote W. Thomas; introduction by Andrew Lang; 1905; anthology_or_compilation; rights public_domain; access fulltext_saved; screen pass; keep; relevance 3, practicality 3, confidence medium (Digest only; the 'milky, black, pictures' sequence comes from the search agent's summary and was not visible in the digest.)
- mode: ambiguous. Thomas and Lang classify crystal pictures as 'pseudo-hallucinations, or objects apparently external but not forming in any real sense part of the outside world', analogous to hypnagogic imagery but seen wide awake. Lang compares them to a speck of colour before shut eyes developing into a face. One account has a dark funnel 'filled with light', i.e. light seen in a dark space without a source. The source itself leaves open whether it is perceptual or imaginal.
- eyes: both; stimulus independence: unclear
- ONSET both: Lang: go alone, back to the light, ball on a dark cloth, exclude reflections, stare about five minutes; if nothing in two or three trials, probably never. Thomas disagrees: Indian Mussulman practice recommends three quarters of an hour or more, and two or three trials are too few. Strong visualisers often fail while others succeed; the faculty can be absent for weeks. Also an account of a first success by looking into a perfectly dark funnel with the head covered, which filled with light before pictures.
- techniques: scrying_surface, gazing_fixed_point, darkness_exposure, sensory_restriction; phenomena: smoke_mist_cloud, white, black_light, light_in_the_room, figures_or_forms
- conditions: posture: sitting, ball in the lap or on a stand; gaze_or_eye_position: stare at the ball at a just focus; setting: alone, back to the light, ball on a dark dress or cloth, reflections excluded; no direct sunlight; duration_and_schedule: five minutes per trial (Lang) up to forty-five minutes or more (Thomas, citing Islamic practice); repeat on different days; prerequisites: no fixed prerequisite; visualisers do not necessarily succeed; other: a dark funnel with the head covered as an alternative first experiment
- stages: milky, then black, then pictures: Per the search agent summary of Lang: the ball grows milky, then black, then pictures; a strong visualiser painter got only the milky or misty phase. -> funnel fills with light: In the dark funnel experiment the funnel filled with light and then fancy pictures followed.
- with the light: Keep gazing; the mist may vanish leaving only the pictures. Record at once.
- obstacles/fixes: Nothing in a few trials: Lang says give up, Thomas says continue far longer | Power absent for weeks even in a gifted scryer | Reflections and points de repere: exclude them if one wants to exclude artefact
- cautions: Moderate indulgence is no more harmful than an after-dinner snooze (Thomas) | Historical Church condemnations of scrying as heresy are quoted
- safety flags: prolonged_darkness
- interpretation: A day-dream-like pseudo-hallucination, allied to visualising, analogous to hypnagogic imagery but occurring in wide-awake people; veridical cases are discussed as telepathy.
- practice: Go alone into a room, sit with your back to the light, and set the glass ball in your lap on a dark cloth so no reflections reach it. Stare at it for about five minutes, thinking of anything you please. Lang says that if two or three trials show nothing you will probably never succeed; Thomas thinks that far too short and cites Islamic instructions to go on for three quarters of an hour or more, noting the power may vanish for weeks. The usual sequence is that the ball grows milky or misty, may go black, and then pictures appear; some strong visualisers get only the mist. An alternative first trial is to look into a perfectly dark funnel with the head covered, which filled with light before images appeared.
  - passage (onset; Ch. III Crystal Visions, source lines 2230-2292): A man looking into a perfectly dark funnel with his head covered found the funnel filled with light, then fancy pictures.
  - passage (method; Ch. IV, Lang's suggestions, source lines 2827-2885): Lang's basic procedure: alone, back to the light, ball on dark cloth, five minutes of staring.
  - passage (onset; Ch. IV, source lines 2827-2885): Thomas disputes Lang's short probation, citing a much longer sitting.
  - passage (testimony; Introduction by Lang, source lines 1370-1450): A painter and strong visualiser saw only milky mist and never pictures.
  - passage (mode; Introduction by Lang, source lines 925-962): Lang likens crystal pictures to hypnagogic faces developing from a speck of colour before shut eyes.
